73 28 ψ 14 36 · 81–85 do di de da · 41–45 ro ri re ra
he r z o g · Herzog Georg · ohne Widerstand ist der Feindt über

Hamburg & Glückstadt → Trauttmansdorff, Ferdinand III, Schwarzenberg · homophonic cipher with a syllable table · Jan–Apr 1639

Kurz von Senftenau to Trauttmansdorff, Hamburg 1639

→

SOA Plzeň, pracoviště Klášter u Nepomuka, Rodinný archiv Trauttmansdorffů, inv. č. 200, kart. 9 · DECODE R3811–R3815, R4625, R4645, R4734, R4736 · 73 images, about 23,000 cipher signs

The key was recovered from a contemporary interlinear decipherment on one page, which shows a regular syllable table, and applied to all nine letters with a language model of early modern German. After two re-readings on the images in October 2026 all nine give German and Latin sentences, with scattered holes; measured on their words, eight reach the 95% bar, and the Glückstadt letter of 14 April (R4736) builds coherent sentences but reads at 94.0%, under it. Jakub Mírka rebuilt the key from the same page in 2012 and summarised that one letter; his key table is on two of the DECODE records. No decipherment of the other eight was found.

Daniel Bourdeau · posted 19 September 2026 · updated 26 October 2026

Method: key recovered based on adjacent plaintext · Extent: partial (100% of the cipher)

Credits · Mírka (earlier solution) · Contemporary key or decipherment (key or decipherment used)

adjacent text
On this pageThe lettersThe keyWhat the letters sayMethodWhat remains uncertainFull textsSources

At a glanceKey from adjacent plaintext · partial (100% of the cipher)

The letters
Nine ciphered reports (Hamburg, January–April 1639) from the imperial vice-chancellor Kurz von Senftenau, mostly to Trauttmansdorff, on the Westphalian peace preliminaries.
Where
Trauttmansdorff family archive, Klášter; nine DECODE records (R3811 to R4736), cipher inline with clear German.
The cipher
Numbers 41–100 are consonant–vowel syllables in a regular table; numbers 1–40 and some signs are single-letter homophones.
How it was read
The key came from the interlinear glosses on the first pages of R3811, with a German language model resolving the rest. A second transcription corrected about 360 symbols.
What it says
Banér’s invasion of Lower Saxony, Arnim’s bid for pardon, the Danish mediation and its collapse, and Kurz’s contempt for “universal” treaties.
Credit
Jakub Mírka rebuilt the key in 2012 from the same glosses but printed neither key nor text.
Still open
Garble where the image does not decide (run-together digits, blots), worst in the Latin passages.

01The letters

DECODEDate and placeToCipher signs
R3811Hamburg, 17 Jan 1639Trauttmansdorff1,613 (p. 1 glossed)
R3813Hamburg, 27 Jan 1639Trauttmansdorff7,799
R3812Hamburg, 3 Feb 1639 (DECODE: 1638)Trauttmansdorff5,526
R3815Hamburg, 6 Feb 1639Ferdinand III860
R4625endorsed 10 Feb 1639Trauttmansdorff2,077
R4645Hamburg, 13 Feb 1639, with clear copies of two Banér lettersTrauttmansdorff1,236
R3814undated, 1639“Hochedler Herr”1,919
R47348 Apr 1639, copySchwarzenberg (per DECODE)480
R4736Glückstadt, 14 Apr 1639Trauttmansdorff1,403

R3812 is dated 1638 in DECODE, but it opens “Mein jüngstes sub dato 27. January”, which is R3813. It is dated Hamburg 3 Feb. 1639 in its clear postscript and reports Arnim in Hamburg after his escape in late 1638. It belongs to 1639.

The cipher runs inline with clear German. Kurz writes the harmless words in the clear and enciphers the names, numbers and judgments.

02The key

About 210 glossed signs on the first two pages of R3811 give the pattern directly: 83 de, 82 di, 84 da; 43 re, 42 ri, 44 ra; 51 no, 52 ni; 72 hi, 74 ha; 93 be; 57 mi, 59 ma. Every ten is split into two halves of five. Each half has one consonant, and its first four places run o i e a:

+1+2+3+4+5
41–45rorirerapu
46–50popipepalu
51–55noninenamu
56–60momimemahu
61–65lolilelaku
66–70kokikekadu
71–75hohihehagu
76–80gogigegabu
81–85dodidedacu
86–90cociceca—
91–95bobibebaus
96–100—isese?ru

The consonants run backwards through the alphabet from 41: r p n m l k h g d c b. The fifth place of each half is a separate series of -u syllables, and each was fixed from words: Reputation and Graf von Puechheim (45), illustrissimi (50), unmüglich (55), Hunger and Churbrandenburg (60), Ankunft (65), durch (70), Herzog Augustus (75), Magdeburg, Meckelburg (80), pericula (85), Haus Österreich (95), zurück, darüber (100).

The numbers 1–40 and some twenty letter and graphic signs are homophones for single letters. The commonest are 28 r, 31 n, 27 s, 26 t, 39 c, 35 h, 18/19/20 e, 17/15/16 i, 21/22/23 a, 10/11/25 u, 12/13/14 o, 36/38 g, π/α m, ψ/φ z, w/#z-tail n and 29 qu. A few stand for pairs: 9 te, 6 se, c si, n ve, d so, k to (corrected from ti on 23 October 2026, after Mírka’s table) and a looped ℓ tu; m is va, and 29 m is qua. A handful of marks are nulls or flourishes. The full table, with counts, is in KEY.md. This is Mírka’s description (“homophonic substitution, bigrams, nulls, no codes”) written out as a table.

03What the letters say

The passages below are quoted from the readings of 22–23 October 2026 (read/<record>_reading.md, with English), in the spelling of the figures and of the clear text; [...] is unread, [?word] partly carried.

17 January (R3811)

Nothing can be done with Duke Georg of Lüneburg: Ohne widerstant ist der feindt über. Recruits are going to Austria and money is short. France, Sweden and Holland are im werck sein, zum konig ain ansehenliche legation zu schicken to Denmark; herzog Augustus von Sachsen Lauenburg wolle sich in dennische protection begeben. Arnheim ist gestert bey mir gewesen: Arnim, suspected over the Friedland affair, swears he plotted nothing against the Peace of Prague, and Kurz advises a Protectorium in optima forma. These are the points of Mírka’s summary.

27 January (R3813), a twenty-page memorandum

Der feindt geht ganz unverwehrt hinuber, ziecht sich gegen Waltzwedel hinauf. Kurz advises a defensive war to keep Austria, Bohemia and Silesia. He distrusts Salvius: Er ist ganz voller gifft contra Domum Austriacam. He calls the universal treaties ein teufflisch inventum et ad ezclusionem omnium tractatuum. France wants Lorraine, Burgundy, Alsace vnnd gar das kaiserthumb; mit Schweden ist umb ein leichters noch zu accordiren, aber gewis nit per Salvium.

3 and 6 February (R3812, R3815)

Halberstat wirdt des Banners raub sein und daß ganze Stifts zu grund gehen. Zwischen Dennemarck und Schweden seindt grosse disgusti, dessen bin ich versichert. As Arnim tells Kurz, Banér’s whole force is zehentusendt mann … ermadtete[s] [...] armseliges hungeriges [?volck]. To the Emperor, Kurz reports that Der Konig in Dennemarck, so viel ich mercke, ist ganz perplex, and the Danes irresolvirt und pusillanimes.

10 and 13 February (R4625, R4645)

A plan to keep the Lower Saxon Circle divided and to hold Halberstadt, Magdeburg, Hildesheim and Wolfenbüttel. The King’s conditions for his mediation have proved empty, and wir wollen uns dannoch und auch allein wehren. Count Pentz says the King must drop the mediation: Ergo ruptura tractatuum hic est principium coniunctionis.

8 and 14 April (R4734, R4736)

In the copy of 8 April, the King has bound hands and per force will er nichts thuen. From Glückstadt, Christian IV’s residence, Kurz writes Von hinnen wolt man mich nit gern dimittiren. He reports the Danish complaint that war in Austria is paid for with peace in Lower Saxony while the Swedes sit in Mecklenburg and Pomerania: Fur solche vormauren geben wir unser schweis und bluet. Pentz ist der gescheidest in dissimuliren.

04Method

Agents transcribed all 73 DECODE images token by token, two at a time. Clear text went in braces and the glosses were attached to their numbers. Plain homophonic annealing with a German 5-gram model (Grimmelshausen and Gutenberg German, early spelling) collapsed into enenen…, even with a letter-frequency penalty. The glosses turned the problem into a structured one: one consonant per half-ten and one value per single sign, seeded with seventeen glossed values. That solver produced fluent German on the first run (ich müesse die Kranckheit beschreiben, damit man sich den remediis darnach zu richten…). The irregular signs were then fixed by checking them in context and by ranking candidate values with the language model. Of the 22,913 transcribed cipher signs, 98.3 % then had a value.

A checked second pass followed on 21 September 2026. Agents compared every line of the 73 transcriptions with crops of the images, working from a printout of each sign’s value, and changed a sign only where the image showed it. About 360 signs were corrected. The commonest slips were a plain c written for the looped sign worth tu, a bold 9 written for the hooked sign worth w, and 6 written for φ (z). In R3813 a crossed ƒ-like sign worth ta had been merged with another sign. A script fault had also dropped 56 cipher lines that began with a graphic sign. The language-model score of the decipherment improved on eight of the nine letters. Of 23,699 cipher signs, 99.9 % now have a value.

05What remains uncertain

  • Residual holes. After the re-readings of 22–23 October 2026 and the check against Mírka’s key, each letter keeps some [?] words and unread runs (R3813 about twenty, R3814 about twelve runs), listed in the notes of each reading file. Where the image does not decide (digits run together, blots, overwritten figures) they stay open. The Latin passages are the weakest.
  • R4736, 14 April. The letter builds coherent sentences but reads at 94.0% of its words, under the 95% bar.
  • Kurz’s own hand. The postscripts and margin notes in his fast cursive (R3811, R3814 p. 4, R4645, R4736) and about twenty clear Kurrent words are not settled at the resolution of the DECODE images.
  • R4645: a leaf between images P2 and P6 is not among the nine DECODE records.
  • α was first taken as m, which gave Salv- before l. The images show two signs: the small upright α is sa (Salvius, caesaris, universal-, Sachsen), and a larger looped form is m. In one hand (R3813) the two shapes are hard to tell apart.
  • 99 is uncertain (e?), 90 occurs once, and 96 does not occur. The signs 3, 5, 7, #s-loop and p are treated as nulls or are unassigned (grade M); 4 is a null except where it is crossed (x, glossed so on R3811 p. 1).
  • The recurring group decrypted as ml in R3812–R3814 is sometimes part of universal- and sometimes a name. It is unresolved.
  • The two Banér copies in R4645 are transcribed: to Lüneburg, 10 January 1639, and to the Lower Saxon envoys, 29 January 1639, refusing to leave the Circle. The endorsement on R4734 names the addressee as Count Schwarzenberg. Kurz’s own clear postscript on R4645 is only half legible.

Full texts

The transcription and the decryption of every document on this page, so that the reading can be used and checked. Short files are shown here; all are in the repository.

R3812, Hamburg, 3 Feb 1639

Kurz von Senftenau on the quartering of six imperial regiments and the Lower Saxon Kreistag: Banér's plundering will ruin the Stift Halberstadt, Duke Georg of Lüneburg will not resist; he sees no real Swedish or French wish for peace and great ill-feeling between Denmark and Sweden. Postscript on Arnim's visit after escaping Swedish captivity.

IMG_R3812_I23123_P.txt

# IMG_R3812_I23123_P  (single page; clear German cover note/postscript, signed, dated "3 feb. 1639"; no cipher)
{Hochgeborner Herr Graf ...}
{clear text only; not transcribed}

Decryption: R3812.txt

Reading: R3812_reading.md

Translation: R3812_en.md

R3811, Hamburg, 17 Jan 1639

Nothing can be done with Duke Georg; France, Sweden and Holland plan an embassy to the King of Denmark to prevent a congress in Sweden; Duke August of Saxe-Lauenburg is said to seek Danish protection; Arnim denies plotting against the Peace of Prague and Kurz advises a protectorium. The letter carries an interlinear decipherment (summarised by Mírka in 2012).

Transcription

IMG_R3811_I23099_P.txt

# IMG_R3811_I23099_P  (single page; letter start, dated 1639 Jan. 17 in margin)
# legend: see tr/LEGEND.md. #z-tail, α (alpha loop), x (x-like crossed sign), c (open c), π, ψ (fork-shaped 9 with long tail), #s-long, #hook
#         π = pi-shaped sign (two uprights with bar); #s-long = long s / f-like tall stroke used as cipher sign
# glosses: interlinear small letters, attached by horizontal position; alignment to a token is approximate where noted '?'
{Hochgeborner Herr Graf, gnädiger Herr,}
{In} 73 28 ψ 14 36 78 13 28 78 w α 39 73 #z-tail 69 31 17 39 35
57 39 35 52 26 23 33 63=l 28=r 82=di w=n 36=g 27 42=r 39=ch 35 9=ten+ 31
12=o 35=h 53=ne #sect=w 15=i 83=de 28=r 27=s 26=t 21=a #z-tail=n 26=t {ist der feindt über,}

R3811_interlinear.md

# R3811: the contemporary interlinear decipherment (transcribed 22 Oct 2026, b06)

Small brown-ink letters written above the figures by a contemporary decipherer, syllable by syllable, on DECODE image
I23099 (p. 1, all cipher lines) and on I23100 left (the first eleven lines). No glosses on I23101 or I23104. Read on
crops of the DECODE images (2x zoom); the glosses are tiny and faded, so single letters marked ? are uncertain. The
clerk wrote the Kurrent e, which looks like n/r/v; it is given here as e where the figure is an e-sign. Gloss
per line, in the order of the figures (figure groups in parentheses where the gloss settles a label); | separates
words as the plain text runs.

## Where gloss and figures disagree, and what the gloss settles

- 4 2 (I23099 ll. 2 and 9) is one group 42 = ri (glossed r / ri): "richten", "virium".
- The hooked p-like sign after #l-loop (I23099 l. 8) is glossed vi; the key gives #hook = a.
- I23099 l. 12: the first 9 is glossed w (#sect), the 0-like sign z (φ): "Weser zu".
- I23099 l. 12, last figure 28 (r) is glossed t: "ist" (a slip of the cipher clerk).
- I23099 l. 14: sign 1 is glossed su ("subsidia"); the key has 1 = i.
- I23099 l. 23: 9 is glossed w (#sect): "wir".
- #s-long is glossed ti (quartir, recrutiren, competiren); the key has t.
- I23100 l. 2: "9 2" is 72 = hi ("Hinterpommeren"); l. 6: "9" is 91 = bo ("Böhmen").

# R3811, Kurz von Senftenau to Trauttmansdorff, Hamburg, 17 Jan 1639

Hochgeborner Herr Graf, gnädiger Herr,

In herzog Georgen sachen kan ich mich nit allerdings richten. Ohne widerstant ist der feindt über, dargegen so viel
Volckh zwar mit verfaulten gewöhrn, so ihn damahls [...] thente, weil und man von neutraliteten aber allher; und
darhier gibt die unterlasene resistenz umb zu viel mehr Argwohn. Ob es ex defectu virium geschicht, und zu
seiner Zeit, und orth albie herum gebraucht würdt, stehet zu erwartten. Elb und Weser zu defendiren ist dem herzog
unmuglich, et nostra subsidia halten sie auch für wenig freündtschafft. Ich wolte, das Salis bessere regimenter bei
sich hette, die sich bei freundt und feindt sehen lassen. [...] ob darunter nit noch etliche reformiert, so doch nit
auf khomen werden; das Stückh von Nidersachsischen Crais haben wir nit aus der posses zu lassen, dem feindt das herüber gehen zu
verbeitten; Scklesien die Quartir leicht zumachen ist mit desterezza von den Luneburgern ein par 1000 Thaler zusammen
gebracht zu haben, und die unberittene eim Ritt machen in Hinterpommeren thuen. Es würdt geschehen aber zu spatt.

In gazetten stehet, recruten sein nach Österreich destiniert. Mit wenig gelt kan man alhie recrutiren, und sie wenden
gern in Bohmen lauffen. Doppelter [...] ist der[...], weil dann was man dort wirbt, is[t] verlohren. Die Herzogen,
so zum feindt khomben, competiren mit den unserigen im hunger leiden, und sein ab schon gebracht; delatiert sich keiner
zu stellen alle die Anschläg. All gelt ist verloren, so nit zu rechter Zeit kombt, und ganz und gar, ein Thaler traidt gilt in 6. Monatt kaum
mehr viel. NB. Von Graf Franzen hab ich noch khein Antwort. Wo Ihr Mtt. ich antreffe, viel-leicht wurdt ihm committirt in polonicis mit
mir zu handlen. Ich avanzier gleichwohl wenig mit Herzogen, allein was beym könig erlebte, [...] und der holsteinis[c]he [...] gesanten rais
nach Luneburg per[i] directum zu befurderen. Potissima causa est, das Franckreich, Schweden und Hollant im werck sein,
zum konig ain ansehenliche legation zu schicken und ihn zu dehortiren, das er ratione congress in Schweden nit der
gestalt tringe und dardurch allen dreien faedera schwechen wolle; ohne zweifel gehen terriculament mit. Darumb solle
[...] ad constantiam Regis demoliert, und den Zoll eingestellt haben.

Addunt: herzog Augustus von Sachsen Lauenburg wolle sich in dennische protection begeben, und die [?Stecknitz]
[?stelle] mit dennischen Volckh besezt werden; it[a] Holsatia in alieno melius et facilius defenditur. Das andere
stehet zu erwartten.

Arnheim ist gestert bey mir gewesen, Consiliatis vermeldt, dolere se, das er bei Kai. Maist in verdacht solte sein, als
wan er vom fridlandischen tradimento gewest, und darfür Cooperiert hette. Wüßte officier so ihn nachgestelt, aber mit
obi befehlen. Wann bey schwelen in hochster gefahr, und also auch der Kai. gnad nit versichert. Mit allem ware
seines herren des churfursten bey vergangenem [...] verstandt intention nit gewesen, einziges Haus, bevorab de[s] Erzhaus
[Ös]ter[r]eich zu ruiniren, sondern er Arnheimb selbst auc[h] [?ir] das in sein instruction gesezt worden, als er zu Fridlant gewest,
zum fall [...] tradiment von ihm werde [...], machts dergleichen sich einzubilden. Wann [?Fridland] Kay. Mtt. untre[u],
so khönte churfurst sich viel weniger was gegen ihm versehen; das mag aber mit ihm tractiert, daran sey die uber aus
grose plenipotenz [?den]. Petiit ut ab [?Electore] repeterem desuper informationem; und wenn daran auß Ihr Kai. Mtt.
informirte seine prothocolla verändert werden, den vorigen Kays. Mtt. hette der churfurst die resolution gehabt, daß
er Arnheim sich stil hielte, und wider pace et bello ursach churfurst habe ad tractatus Ihm occasion wollen auß
handen lassen, den pragerischen friden nichts machiniere, er alhie Kays. schuz versichert solte sein.

Ich habe ihm gegen leistung seiner schuldigkeit ain ebenmesig bey [...] Kays. Mtt. versichert, et demisi illum
contentum. Mit meinem Rath gibt man ihm ein protectorium in optima forma und obligirt ihn; er ist doch in der
amnistia, nisi probentur fridlandica. Und wie wurdt man dises, et quo commodo, erheben? Wafen bekombt er doch nochmals,
vel pro nobis vel contra nos. Engeland und Franckreich machen ihm alhie caresses, die Schweden auch wider; sagt alle
suspiciones zu vermeiden wolle er von hinnen weck. Petiit a me ein pass nach Preussen. Wie ist besser ein
protectorium von Kai. [...] Maii.? Helt er sich demselben gemes, zu sehen.

Wohl angelegt. Im übrigen thue ich bey Dennemarck gern das meinige. [?Arnheim] [...]: Denemarckh konde
nit aus dem spill sein. Wir habe ihm mehr das glück und unglück zum fenster hinein gesehen. Stockholmb sei in seinen
handen, wan er nur wolle kriegen; Schweden Pommeren [...] dagegen verlohren. Putat per modum der Crais
verfassung [?ettlis], das der feind verfolgt und [...] reichsboden getriben werde, konte für am besten in de[s] spil gebracht
werden. Svecos non inter Holsatiae [?ducem] et regem Daniae non distinguere, idque regia suecis responsum cum nuper
rescripsisset: er als ein herzog von Holstein und ein Reichsfurst konde dem werck also lenger nit zusehen;
defendi contrarium; calamittes telli, und wer fridt haben konne, wie ihn die exempla aliorum deterriren. Er ist
aber bey seinen opinion, und der discurs bey dem verbleiben.

Hamburg den 17. January 1639.

[Postscript in Kurz's own cursive, clear German, not transcribed.]

## Notes

- Interlinear (I23099 and I23100 left ll. 1-11): the glosses are the clerk's syllable-by-syllable decipherment and
  agree with the key decode. They carry: "richten" (the group 4 2 is 42 = ri, glossed r), "Weser" (9 glossed w =
  #sect, 0 glossed z = φ), "subsidia" (sign 1 glossed su; the key's 1 = i), "[vi]rium" (the hooked sign after #l-loop
  is glossed vi; the key's #hook = a), "Hinterpommeren" (9 2 = 72, hi), "in Bohmen" (9 = 91, bo), "competren" (the
  gloss reads c-o-m-pe-ti-re-n). The gloss over the last figure of "unterlasene resistenz" is tz (φ).
- Key of 22 Oct 2026 (38 = d, Δ = nu, #dot = su, #s-long = ti, 9v = vo) applied; decode.py R3811 re-run. "9" read
  vo (label 9v) where te makes no word and vo makes the word: man von neutraliteten, bevorab, Augustus von Sachsen,
  von hinnen, protectorium von Kai. Mai., herzog von Holstein (read/R3811_corrections.tsv).
- Aligner (b06_align.py) flags 7 words, all explained: "ex", "virium", "ist" (Weser line) and "subsidia" are carried by
  the interlinear gloss over figures the key does not value so (4+ = x, #hook = vi, 28 glossed t, 1 = su);
  "protectorium" (twice) and "Stockholmb" need x = to, which the key does not have (x is several signs merged: i, to,
  su, vi, ta; see the R4736 log).
- The "Extract Schreibens aus Hamburg vom 18 January 1639" on I23102/I23103 is a separate clear document laid over
  the spread, not part of this letter.
- Open: the clear text at "[...]" (faded or cramped Kurrent that the first transcription left out and this pass did
  not settle: "Doppelter [...] ist der[...]", "[...] ob darunter", "bey [...] Kays. Mtt.", "Darumb solle [...]", the word after "bey vergangenem"),
  and the postscript in Kurz's cursive.
- Second pass (parent session, 22 Oct 2026): clear text re-read on the images (so viel Volckh, Argwohn, Ich wolte,
  etliche reformiert so doch nit, von den Luneburgern ... zusammen gebracht zu haben, was man dort wirbt, Die Herzogen
  so zum feindt khomben ... leiden, traidt, Graf Franzen, antreffe, mit Herzogen allein was beym, Darumb solle,
  verstandt, einziges Haus); I23101 l. 12 re-read: "93 9 44 40 ... 28 φ 74 95 96 9 43 17 39 35" = "bevorab de[s] Erzhaus
  [Ös]terreich" (9 = vo in bevorab; 96, unvalued in the key, = Ös). Key now carries nu, su and ti.
- Parent session, 22 Oct: protectorium (twice) and Stockholmb printed [?] (x = to is not in the key; nothing else carries them).

# R3811, Kurz von Senftenau to Trauttmansdorff, Hamburg, 17 Jan 1639

Most high-born Lord Count, gracious Lord,

In Duke Georg's affairs I cannot quite find my way. The enemy is across without resistance; against him [there are]
so many troops, though with rotten muskets, such as [...] did at that time, because one [hears] of neutralities here;
and here the failure to resist gives all the more suspicion. Whether it happens for lack of strength, and will be used
at its time and place hereabouts, remains to be seen. To defend the Elbe and the Weser is impossible for the Duke, and
they also take our subsidies for little friendship. I would wish Salis had better regiments with him, that could show
themselves to friend and foe. [...] whether among them some are not still reformed, which yet will not come up; the
part of the Lower Saxon Circle we must not let out of our possession, [and must] forbid the enemy to cross; to make
the quarters in Silesia easy is [done] with dexterity, having collected a couple of thousand thalers from the
Lüneburgers, and the unmounted [men] make a raid into Farther Pomerania. It will happen, but too late.

The gazettes say recruits are destined for Austria. With little money one can recruit here, and they would gladly run
to Bohemia. Double [...] is the [...], because what one recruits there is lost. The dukes who go over to the enemy
compete with ours in suffering hunger, and are already brought down; no one denounces himself to stand by all the
plans. All money is lost that does not come at the right time, and entirely: a thaler of grain is worth hardly
anything in six months. NB. From Count Franzen I still have no answer. If I meet His Majesty, perhaps he will be
commissioned to deal with me in Polish affairs. I make little progress all the same with the dukes, only what [I]
saw at the King's, [...] and to forward the Holstein [...] envoys' journey to Lüneburg directly. The chief cause is
that France, Sweden and Holland are about to send the King a considerable embassy and dissuade him from pressing so
hard for the congress in Sweden and thereby weakening the alliances of all three; no doubt threats go with it.
Therefore [...] shall have been demolished at the King's instance, and the toll stopped.

They add: Duke August of Saxe-Lauenburg wants to put himself under Danish protection, and the [?Stecknitz]
[?position] be garrisoned with Danish troops; thus Holstein is better and more easily defended on another's ground.
The rest remains to be seen.

Arnim was with me yesterday, [and] reported [...] that he grieves that he should be suspected by His Imperial Majesty,
as if he had been [part] of the Friedland treason, and had cooperated in it. [He] knew officers who pursued him, but
with orders from above. When at [?…] in the greatest danger, and so also not assured of the Emperor's grace. With all
this it was not the intention of his lord the Elector at the past [...] understanding to ruin a single house, least of
all the Archducal House of Austria, but that he, Arnim, himself was put into his instruction when he was with
Friedland, in case [...] treason was [...] by him, [and] to imagine the like. If [?Friedland] were disloyal to His
Imperial Majesty, the Elector could expect much less from him; that may be negotiated with him, [and with it] the
exceedingly great plenipotentiary power [...]. He asked that I should ask the [?Elector] for information on it; and if
His Imperial Majesty, informed of it, changed his protocols: at the late Emperor's [time] the Elector had resolved
that he, Arnim, should keep quiet, and against peace and war the Elector had cause [and] would let slip the occasion
of negotiations, machinate nothing against the Peace of Prague, [and] he should be assured of imperial protection
here.

I have assured him of the same with [...] His Imperial Majesty against the performance of his duty, and sent him away
content. On my advice one gives him a protectorium in the best form and binds him; he is after all within the
amnesty, unless Friedland matters are proved. And how will one raise this, and with what convenience? He will get
arms again, either for us or against us. England and France court him here, the Swedes too; he says he wants to go
away from here to avoid all suspicions. He asked me for a pass to Prussia. How much better a protectorium from His
Imperial [...] Majesty? Whether he behaves according to it remains to be seen.

Well invested. For the rest I gladly do my part with Denmark. [?Arnim] [...]: Denmark could not stay out of the game.
We have rather watched fortune and misfortune through the window. Stockholm would be in his hands if he only wanted
to make war; Sweden would lose Pomerania [...] for it. He thinks that by way of the Circle's arrangement [...], so
that the enemy is pursued and driven [...] imperial soil, [it] could best be brought into play. The Swedes do not
distinguish between the [?Duke] of Holstein and the King of Denmark, and the royal answer to the Swedes said so when
he wrote back lately: he, as a Duke of Holstein and a prince of the Empire, could not look on at the business any
longer; to defend the contrary; calamities [...], and whoever could have peace, as the examples of others deter him.
But he holds to his opinion, and the discussion rests there.

Hamburg, 17 January 1639.

[Postscript in Kurz's own cursive, clear German, not transcribed.]

R3813, Hamburg, 27 Jan 1639

Twenty-page memorandum: the enemy advances unopposed towards Salzwedel; a Kreistag broken up by the enemy is better than one concluded with express neutrality; Kurz advises a defensive strategy, distrusts Salvius, weighs a secret particular treaty with Sweden through Brandenburg or Denmark, and says France wants Lorraine, Alsace and even the imperial crown.

Transcription

R3814, undated, 1639

The Swedish agent Mithoff and the Danish mediation; safe-conducts; Lorraine, Mantua and Pomerania in pledge; Rákóczi said to take French money with the Turk's consent; Kurz defends his secret particular dealing with Mithoff and describes a secret post route via Ehrenberg and Wolfenbüttel.

contemporary decipherment, gloss or edition is known. Spelling as the figures and the clear hand give it; words

# R3814, Kurz von Senftenau to Trauttmansdorff, undated, 1639

Hochedler herr und gnediger herr. Nachdem [...] drei posten des Mithofi und das Salvii zu Leipseig ohne mein [...]
allhero bringen lassen, und erofnet er mir zugestellt: die schreiben sein meistentheils in ziffer, per discretion. Ob
ich aber zimblich weit hineinkomben, mit [...] herumber in lamentis, das man der [...] abzuarbeiten: dant
culpam mihi, rogant Salvium et sperant ab illo meliora; und dieser verhaist ihm [...] wollte nichts thuen, biss die
wieder komen. Additur, Schweden werde dergestalt sich von Denemarck keine leges praescribiren lassen, und ad Saluos Conductus
zwingen, wie er wolle. Uberschickt, das Dennemarck hierin redlich gearbeitet habe. Sed quod de caetero nulla sit spes
nec diei denominade.

[...] sie es iure feudi [...] wolten haben, obs nit allein auf des [?Gustav] und der iezigen Königin erben zu
bringen; corona enim est immortalis. 5. Ob es nit hoc casu beider simultinea investitura, wie bey Pommeren mit
Brandenburg, zu erhalten; und dies sol satisfactionem coronae betrift. Der passus wegen contentirung der
soldatesca mues ausbleiben, ist umbsonst [...]; [...] wer heruber gehet, bekombt gelt; amnistia stehet bei Ihr Kay. Mt.
suo tempore. Das groste ist, ob uns Schweden was restituieren konde, [...] Elsas et illorum quae occupat post [...]
faedus in praedictis litteris Salui schickt. NB Brisacum erit Suecorum; es ist immediate darwider. Ihrem vorthel haben
aber wir des Ungarischen Khönigreichs [...] ipsis restituimus, quod prius [...] habeant, ausser dem in khönden also
nit gefert worden loca restituenda, wenn also zu specificiren, et omnia ad manus Caesaris. NB zu consultiren, was
Dennemarck fur interesse bei Riga hat; und in somma in hohster hehster gehaimb ein proiect auf [...], wie mann
vermainte das mit disen leuthen zu schliessen. Nichts falschers hab ich mein lebtag nit gesehen; alle wort [...] zu
ponderiren, ob der ihm mit zu witleuftigkeit dienen, modum [?tractandi] iam sine salvis conductibus
generalissimis, item hollandicis et aliis, die ihm noch einfallen möchten.

Im übrigen erscheint das Salvius den Mithofium foppe, allegando exempli gratia, mann haisse den herzog Bernhart [...]
die landgrafin Elisabeth wie ein knecht und ein diern; herzog Bernhart werde doch auch das dennische schreiben albereit,
die cron remittiren, Burgund, Elsas, Lothringen, [?Savoia], Mantua gar, wer ein der Franzosen handen und also in ihrer
macht, weil zu restituiren. Subnectit tandem, tractatibus universalibus pacem sperare posse. Man wolte ihn ein bott
weegen Pommeren thuen. Denn findt ich auss Balbey disen schreiben, quod Salvius ludat in hoc negotio, et quod nihil
tam absurdi petat Mithovius de aula Caesarea sibi non promittat. Darnach ist zwar dem Werck auss [...] nichts wurdt,
nit vil geschadet, aber auch nichts genuzt; aber a proposito dieser, was F. H. Ungern Grafen von Schwrzenberg
erinnert haben. Hiermit haben die ein wer dessen dem churfursten von mir communicirten aufsaz: ezaminetur parum
punctus satisfactionis. Primo, ob es auf ein stuck landts iure pignoris zu bringen cum denominatione summae. Secundo,
wan es halb Pomeren sein solte, obs [...] vor oder hinter Pomeren zu lassen, [...] an hinter Pomeren fort solle; obs
nit auf [...] zu bringen. Mehr consilia erfahrt man also in eim tag als sonst in iahren. Quaeritur, obs geschehen
solle. Ragozi solle mit consens des Turckhens gelt von Franckreich empfangen haben, und also zur nachricht.

[...] scripsi; es ist aber bei [?dem] gleichen die materia auch mir mit N.N. zu entwerffen, und dann die forma in verbis
also, das nichts ubrigs were als das kindt zu taufen. Ich hette mir lengst disen modum particularem nit misfallen
lassen, [...] cum Mithofio arcana tractare, der nit allein nit schweigt, sonder glorirt de prerepta palma. Dennemarck,
ehe mann noch anhebt zu tractiren, est et fuit impossibile. Wann mir dise herren nur folgen wolten, ich wolte sie wol
gebracht haben; es ist ihnen und dann knecht aber unmuglich, dies gar unverfenglich; dieses korn zeit [...]; daran mit
Schweden unmuglich zu schliesen. Der vorarbeit, wan sie in geheimb bleibt, kan nit schaden, etiam cum mea
universalitate distincta. Komb ich mit hart gezwungen mit der anderen [...] bede, nichts werth ad pacem, wohl zu einem
krieg bis an jüngsten tag. Dan sicherer gelegenheit folgen die schreiben [...], so viel möglich. Absonderliche
correspondenzen mit Salvio gehen aus den Erblanden alhero uber Nurenberg mit [?der] selbe post, am besten nider zu
werffen; die Wolffenbutlischen thun dies ob thun, ganz unvermerckt; man lest hernach die Wehselbrief sein Ambtmann
finden, der erhebt noch ein grosen schaz und schickts forth.

## Notes

- Signs: the crossed long-s is ta in tam, petat, promittat, tractatibus, tractare, prerepta, universalitate, distincta,
  zu taufen, eim tag, Gusta- (relabelled #f-cross) and vi in Salvius/-ii/-o/-um/salvis, Mithovius, nit vil (relabelled pv,
  key pv = vi, 22 Oct); `Salui` (P2, 25 = u) confirms Salvius. `9` reads vo (9v) in vorthel, von Schwarzenberg, von mir,
  gelt von Franckreich, Vorarbeit.
- The points are numbered in the cipher ("4.", "5." with a raised mark); the "5." on p. 2 had been read as the sign 5.
- Unread: runs that do not decode into words although the figures are legible (P1 "c 93 28" before drei posten,
  "befhbe", "der soubel"; P2 "w?ln" at the head, "rirettransac", "aber teir", "quano snisn lipsis", "lun habeant",
  "wort lo in"; P3 "auss dem ecich", "obs nuzerter", "auf terte freltung benstettin"; P5 "lece", "zeit wetlohren",
  "der anderen lo in"; "deziferirter"), and the Latin of P2.
- Postscript, p. 4: about twenty lines in Kurz's own fast, abbreviated hand below the cipher, signed "Unterthänigster
  ... Kurtz". Read only in part at DECODE resolution ("Der Wolfenbüttler ... die schreiben ... folgen ... in guetem
  secreto haben: die schreiben zum theil das Vertrauen zu den Nidersächsischen Craisstendt, zum theil die friedts
  intention ... particular ... Versicherung, quod me terreret ... difficultates moviert ... ander particular ...
  Versicherung"); recorded in tr/ as an editorial note and left out of the body.
- Mithoff (the Swedish agent at Hamburg), Salvius (the Swedish plenipotentiary), Duke Bernhard of Saxe-Weimar,
  Landgravine Amalie Elisabeth of Hesse-Kassel, Rákóczi (Ragozi) and Balbey are named; the secret correspondence runs
  from the hereditary lands via Nuremberg with the same post, through Wolfenbüttel, the bill of exchange left for the
  bailiff to find.
Translation

<!-- English translation of read/R3814_reading.md (Claude Opus 5.5, 22 Oct 2026, batch b06, second pass). Same holes. -->

# R3814, Kurz von Senftenau to Trauttmansdorff, undated, 1639: English

Most noble lord and gracious lord. After [...] three posts of Mithoff and the letter of Salvius at Leipzig had been
brought here without my [...], and opened, he handed them to me: the letters are mostly in cipher, at discretion.
Whether I have got fairly far with [?Mithoff], round about in laments, that one [...] to work off: they lay the blame
on me, they ask Salvius and hope for better from him; and he promised him [...] would do nothing until they came
again. It is added that Sweden will in this way let Denmark prescribe no laws to it, and force the safe-conducts as he wishes.
Sent over [the news] that Denmark had worked honestly in this. But that for the rest there is no hope, nor of naming
the day.

[...] they would have it by feudal right [...], whether not to bring it on the heirs of [?Gustavus] and the present
Queen alone; for the crown is immortal. 5. Whether it could not be kept, in this case, by a simultaneous investiture of
both, as with Pomerania and Brandenburg; and this concerns the satisfaction of the crown. The passage about
contenting the soldiery must be left out, it is in vain [...]; [...] whoever comes over gets money; the amnesty rests
with His Imperial Majesty in its own time. The greatest [question] is whether Sweden could restore anything to us,
[...] Alsace and those which it occupies after [...] the treaty, in the said letters of Salvius he sends. NB Breisach
will be the Swedes'; it is directly against it. To their advantage, however, we [have] of the Kingdom of Hungary [...] we
restore to them, which they [...] before [...] have; besides which, places to be restored could not be [...] if they are to be
specified thus, and everything into the Emperor's hands. NB to consult what interest Denmark has in Riga; and in sum,
in highest secrecy, to [...] a project, as one thought to conclude it with these people. I have never in my life seen
anything falser; to weigh every word [...], whether he [...] with too much prolixity, the way of [?treating] now
without most general safe-conducts, also Dutch ones and others that may still occur to him.

For the rest it appears that Salvius fools Mithoff, alleging for example that one calls Duke Bernhard [...] the
Landgravine Elisabeth like a servant and a maid; Duke Bernhard will after all already [...] the Danish letter, the
crown remit Burgundy, Alsace, Lorraine, [?Savoy], Mantua entirely, [which] are in the hands of the French and so in
their power, because [they are] to be restored. He adds at last that one can hope for peace from universal treaties.
One wanted to make him an offer about Pomerania. Then I find from Balbey's letter that Salvius is playing in this
business, and that there is nothing so absurd Mithoff might ask that he does not promise himself from the imperial
court. After that nothing came of the work from [...], [it] did not do much harm, but no good either; but by the
way, what Count of Schwarzenberg reminded [about] Hungary. Herewith [...] the paper communicated by me to the Elector:
let the point of satisfaction be examined a little. First, whether it is to be brought onto a piece of land by right of
pledge, naming the sum. Second, if it is to be half of Pomerania, whether [...] Hither or Further Pomerania is to be left, [...]
should continue behind Pomerania; whether it is not to be brought to [...]. So one learns more counsels in one day than
otherwise in years. It is asked whether it should happen. Rákóczi is said to have received money from France with the
Turk's consent; and so for information.

[...] I wrote; but it is in the same [way] the matter to be drafted with me too with N.N., and then the form in words,
so that nothing would remain but to baptise the child. I would long since not have disliked this particular way, [...]
to treat secrets with Mithoff, who not only does not keep silent but boasts of the palm snatched [from] Denmark; before
one even begins to treat, it is and was impossible. If these lords would only follow me, I would have brought them
well; it is impossible for them, and then [the] servant, this quite harmless; this grain time [...]; on it impossible
to conclude with Sweden. The preparatory work, if it stays secret, can do no harm, even with my universality kept
distinct. If I come, hard pressed, with the other [...] both, worth nothing for peace, rather for a war until the Last
Day. Then, at a safer opportunity, the letters will follow [...], as far as possible. Separate correspondence with
Salvius goes from the hereditary lands here via Nuremberg with [?the] same post, best sent down; those of
Wolfenbüttel do this, quite unnoticed; afterwards one lets his bailiff find the bill of exchange, who collects a great
treasure besides and sends it on.

R3815, Hamburg, 6 Feb 1639, to Ferdinand III

The King of Denmark is perplexed and perhaps stalling to avoid a conjunction with the Emperor; the Danish Reichsrat is irresolute; with 8-9,000 men the princes could deal with a starved, exhausted enemy; Salis sent to Hildesheim, Duke Georg, the Kreis resolution, Hatzfeldt.

SUMMARY_R3815.md

# R3815: summary

## 1. Date, place, addressee
- **Place/date:** "Datum Hamburg den 6. Febru. Ao. 1639" (clear text, p.2 right).
- **Addressee:** "Allergnedigister Kaiser und Herr", so Emperor Ferdinand III. The subscription reads "E. K. M. Allerunterthänigster ..." and the signature is not transcribed.
- **Pages:** p.1 and p.2 (the body, ending in the date), and p.3, a cipher-heavy sheet that continues a letter, probably an enclosure or continuation. P.4 is blank.

## 2. Summary
Kurz tells the Emperor that his earlier letter has certainly arrived and that he is sending the answer and what was resolved after it. He does not think it necessary to answer point by point: his own relations will explain matters, and so will what General-Wachtmeister Count Puchheim and (Hans?) Mandelslo put to Banér. On Denmark he writes that, as far as he can tell, the King is "ganz perplex" and bound (obligat) to aim for peace, and that the Danes may drag their feet so as to escape a *conjunction*, meaning an alliance with the Emperor. The Danish Reichsrat and the estates are irresolute and faint-hearted (*pusillanimes*) after the "accident", so little of substance can be hoped for. Kurz has still had no answer from Vienna about the Holstein complaint. The King of Denmark has also made a declaration to the English envoy.

The p.3 sheet is about stopping the enemy (the Swedes) from crossing over and shipping troops. It says that if "their princely graces" (probably Duke Georg of Brunswick-Lüneburg) resolved to act with some 8-9,000 men against an exhausted, starving enemy, much could be done, though "vox populi" says otherwise. It asks whether the Emperor wants General-Feldzeugmeister Salis, or another commander, sent to Hildesheim with imperial letters. Kurz is waiting for Salis's final answer at Hamburg about the Danish arrival, in the presence of the English envoy, and wants Duke Georg brought in. It also mentions the pass and troops going after the Kreis resolution (*Kreisschluss*), and Field Marshal Count Hatzfeldt.

## 3. Sample passages
1. [nit erachtet ewer Maiestät dienst zu ... alles ... was darinnen begriffen ... zu beantworten] "(I have) not thought it (necessary) for Your Majesty's service to answer everything contained therein." The stretch "zulolin allessoga rgenapa" is garbled.
2. [der König in Dennemarck ... ist ganz perplex ... obligat ... zum Friden] "The King of Denmark, as far as I can tell, is quite perplexed ... obliged ... to peace."
3. [der Reichsrath und [Stände] ... des Accidents halber irresolut und pusillanimes und schwerlich etwas Hauptsächliches zu hoffen] "The Council of the Realm and the estates ... irresolute and faint-hearted because of the accident, and hardly anything substantial to be hoped for."
4. [so hetten sie mit einem ... acht oder neun ... [tausend] Mann ... und darzu noch ermatteten und erhungerten Feind zu thun] "then they would have (with) some eight or nine (thousand) men ... and moreover an exhausted and starved enemy to deal with." "ahoinneun ulintmann" is corrupt.
5. [mit kaiserlichen Schreiben nach Hildesheim ab[schicken]] "(send) to Hildesheim with imperial letters." Also [dass ich zu Herzog Georgen kommen könnte] "so that I could come to Duke Georg."

## 4. Confidence
About 65-70% of the cipher reads as coherent German. It is sound where it names things: Denmark, Reichsrat, Hildesheim, Herzog Georg, Hatzfeldt, "pusillanimes". It breaks down in p.1 l.3-4 ("zulolinallessoga rgenapa"), in "puschwritficirtt", "wafenallon?nrung", "zuloloechungdesgraventenhalefelgs", "stusrerumveren" and "tenachtteahoinneun ulintmann". The clear text is itself only roughly transcribed, and parts of p.2 right are elided.
Reading

second pass by the parent session on full-resolution crops of every cipher line of P1-P3). It rests on the key decode
(key.json as corrected 22 Oct 2026: 38 = d, Δ = nu, #dot = su, #s-long = ti, m = a) of tr/IMG_R3815_*.txt after the
corrections in read/R3815_corrections.tsv; the clear German was re-read on the images where the reading needed it. No
contemporary decipherment, gloss or edition is known. Page order: image P1 right, P3 left and right, P2 left and right.
In this hand (the letter to the Emperor) #s-long, #dot, p and λ often stand as flourishes before c, h, g and t (sach,
furst, absonderlich, erfolgenden, Herzog) and are read as nulls there. Spelling as the figures and the clear hand give
it; words separated and punctuation added. Clear and deciphered text are not marked apart (read/R3815.txt shows which
is which). Marks: [...] unread; [?word] partly carried; [x] letters or a word supplied. -->

# R3815, Kurz von Senftenau to Ferdinand III, Hamburg, 6 Feb 1639

Allergnedigister Kaiser und Herr. Mein an [...] abgangenes schreiben haben E. Kay. Mt. jüngst ohne Zweifel
allergnedigst empfangen. Hiermit kombt die Antwort, und was sich ferner darauf replicirt, hab nit erachtet ewer
Maiestat dienst zu sein, alles so gar [?genau], was darinnen begriffen, [?also] zu beantworten. Meine wenige Relationes,
wie auch was General Wachtmeister Graf von Puchheimb und der von Mandelslo dem Baner vor und angebracht, werden aller
mehr als zuviel erleutern, die ganze Kai. armada wegen erwartung des Craiss [?schlus] crepieren zu lassen. Hat sich mit
woll thun lassen, dan uber frohrener Elbe hette wol ohne sondere resistenz der ubergang dem feindt nit [?thun]
verhindert werden. Ob aber ein ebenmessige macht von nothen genug were, in dem der feindt zu schif nach und nach
hiruber [...], daran zweifle ich. So steht aber nun ein geschechene sach; und wann annoch ihre furst. gnaden mit
zuziechung des Graven von Hazfelds zu was sich resolviren wolten, so hetten sie mit einem von acht bis in neun
tausent mann und darzu noch ermatten und erhungerten feindt zu thuen. [...] ist vox populi alhier, und bringen es
[...], so w[a]s zu recognosciren ausgeschickt, mit sich.

Allerunterthänigst stelle E. Kays. Mt. ich anheimb, ob sie den General Feldzeugmeister Sales oder commendo andern mit
[...] kaiserlichen schreiben nacher Hildesheimb absonderlich vorgesehen lassen wollen, nicht als albereit geschechene,
sonderen das kunftige zu verhinderen. Ich wolte mich lebsten dahin befurderen, so muss ich aber der endlichen Antwort
dess Salvij alhier, [...] quam fundamento der dennischen kunftigen [...] haben, thun oder lang erfolgenden resolution
erwarten, die dan nicht weniger auch dem Craisstag bey wohnen, sein auch mit versehent, weil sich der status rerum
verenderen mochte, das ich zu Herzog Georgen komben konte; sonsten aber, die mit der pas und die zeit erleidet, nach
geschlossenem Craistag dahin wegfertig[en]. Der Salis wurdt hieran ihrer furst. gnaden ohne das aufwarten, und erinnere
obiges bloss, ihn mehrers zu accreditieren. So hat auch E. Kays. Mtt. General Feldmarschalckh Graf von Hazfeldt
anzeitlicher erinnerung und ob

[...] nicht [...] manglen lassen. Der Konig in Dennemarck, so viel ich mercke, ist ganz perplex, [...] obligation darnach zu trachten, [...]gen in
salvium, und herauss [?pressung], das sie kein intention zum friden haben, damit sich wohl gebührt, lest, ob mit
Dennemarckh zur desto geschwinderen entfliechung der coniunction tergiversiren möchte. [...]ficirt der Reichsrath und
standt bey E. Kays. Mtt. wafen [...], und des accidents [...] irresolvirt und pusillanimes, und schwerlich [?etwas]
haubtsachlichs zu hofen, biss man sich allergnedigst widerumb zu veldt [...]lest.

Auch mein schreiben wegen der holsteinischen Beschwerde abermahls nach Lüneburg hab ich noch khein antwort bekommen,
und ist auch Graf Franz [...] bescheide noch nit angelangt. Wass sich Ihre Königl. Würd. zu Dennemarckh derwegen gegen
dem Englischen Envoyé alhier resolvirt, dessgibt der beyschluss; und ich thue mich [...] E. Kays. Mtt. zu beharlichen
Kays. gnaden allerunterthänigst empfehlen. Datum Hamburg den 6. Febru. Ao. 1639. E. K. M. [...]

## Notes

- Second pass (parent session) on the crops: `37 λ 41` (not #l-loop): "uber frohrener Elbe hette" (the Elbe frozen);
  P3 `φ 16 19` (was 61 61 19): "mit zuziechung des Graven von Hazfelds" (φ also in Hazfelds and Herzog); P3 `ψ`
  (was the 4-hook): "die mit der pas und die zeit erleidet"; `27 p` before n d t = #f-cross: "standt".
- `9` reads vo three times here (von nothen, Graven von Hazfelds, einem von acht): a polyphonic 9 of the same shape
  as 9 = te, entered in key.json as 9v = vo (22 Oct 2026) and relabelled 9v in tr/ (see R3815_corrections.tsv).
- `p` reads vi in "resolviren", "irresolvirt", "sal[v]um" (the key has p as a null; elsewhere it is a flourish); 23 Oct 2026 (r2c): the Salvium p is the curled p-form, relabelled pv (vi), now "salvium".
- Clear text re-read on the images: "dess Salvij alhier" (Salvius, not Salis), "auch dem Craisstag bey wohnen", "weil sich
  der status rerum", "So hat auch", "Hazfeldt", and the close on P2 right ("Graf Franz ... bescheide noch nit angelangt.
  Wass sich Ihre Königl. Würd. ... resolvirt, dessgibt der beyschluss").
- Still unread (figures legible, no outside blocker; one occurrence each, no value fits): "lo in" before obligation,
  "trin n(so)" before -gen in salvum, "pusch-w-ri" before -ficirt, "allon ? n ru ng" after wafen, "samre hin" before
  irresolvirt, "kin" before -lest, "tu" after mit, the head of the "quam fundamenti" clause, the clear word before "ist
  vox populi" and after "bringen es".

<!-- English translation of read/R3815_reading.md (Claude Opus 5.5, 22 Oct 2026, batch b06). Same holes. -->

# R3815, Kurz von Senftenau to Ferdinand III, Hamburg, 6 Feb 1639: English

Most gracious Emperor and Lord. Your Imperial Majesty has no doubt lately most graciously received my letter sent off
[...]. Herewith comes the answer, and what has been further replied to it; I have not thought it Your Majesty's service
to answer everything it contains so [?exactly]. My few reports, and also what Sergeant-Major-General Count Puchheim and
Mandelslo put and brought before Banér, will explain more than enough [how] the whole imperial army [is] left to perish
while waiting for the Circle's [?resolution]. It could well have been done, for over the frozen Elbe the enemy's
crossing could hardly have been prevented without particular resistance. But whether an equal force would have been
[?needed], since the enemy [came] over here little by little by ship, that I doubt. But it is now a thing that has
happened; and if His Princely Grace, with the participation of Count Hatzfeldt, had still been willing to resolve
on something, they would have had to deal with one of eight to nine thousand men and moreover with an exhausted and
starved enemy. [...] is the voice of the people here, and they bring [...] with them, what was sent out to
reconnoitre.

I most humbly leave it to Your Imperial Majesty whether you will have General of Ordnance Salis, or another by command,
provided particularly with [...] imperial letters to Hildesheim, not about what has already happened but to prevent
what is to come. I would most gladly have gone there myself, but I must await the final answer of Salvius here,
[...] of the foundation of the future Danish [...], or the resolution that will follow before long, which [they] will
attend at the Circle diet no less; they are also provided [with it], since the state of affairs might change so that
I could come to Duke Georg; otherwise, as far as the pass and the time allow, [I shall] set out there after the Circle
diet has closed. Salis will in any case attend His Princely Grace on this, and I mention the above merely to accredit
him further. Your Imperial Majesty has also [given] Field Marshal General Count Hatzfeldt timely reminder, and whether

[...] not [...] let be lacking. The King of Denmark, as far as I notice, is quite perplexed, [...] obligation to strive
for it, [...] into safety, and [?pressing] [him to declare] that they have no intention of peace, as is fitting, lets
[it be seen] whether he might temporise with Denmark, the sooner to escape the conjunction. [...] the Council of the
Realm and the estates [...] Your Imperial Majesty's arms [...], and of the accident [...] irresolute and faint-hearted,
and hardly [?anything] substantial to be hoped for, until one most graciously again [...] into the field.

Also on my letter about the Holstein complaint, again to Lüneburg, I have received no answer yet, and Count Franz's
[...] reply has not arrived either. What His Royal Dignity of Denmark has resolved on this towards the English envoy
here, the enclosure shows; and I [...] most humbly commend myself to Your Imperial Majesty's continued imperial grace.
Given at Hamburg, 6 February 1639. Your Imperial Majesty's [...]

R4625, Hamburg, 10 Feb 1639, to Trauttmansdorff

Memorandum on keeping the Lower Saxon Circle divided and holding Halberstadt, Magdeburg, Hildesheim and Wolfenbüttel; 8,000 Danish men in Holstein; three grades of Circle war; the imperial army is ruined.

Transcription

SUMMARY_R4625.md

# R4625: summary

## 1. Date, place, addressee
- **Addressee:** "Hochgeborner Herr, Gnädiger Herr" (p.7) and "E. H." throughout, which fits Trauttmansdorff.
- **Date:** no date line is legible in the transcription. The clear text on p.7 mentions "22. Jan.", so the letter is late January or early February 1639. The place is not shown but is probably Hamburg.
- **Pages:** pp.2, 3, 5 and 6 carry a long memorandum. P.7 is a separate covering letter. Pp.1 and 4 are blank.

## 2. Summary
This is a political memorandum on the Lower Saxon Circle and how to keep it on the Emperor's side against the Swedes. Kurz argues that the Circle should be kept divided (*den Crais disjunct zu halten*) and that the House of Saxony (Brunswick-Lüneburg) should be kept in check. Halberstadt, Magdeburg, Hildesheim and Wolfenbüttel should be held so the Empire keeps its authority. Troops should be fed "head for head" (*Kopf für Kopf*) from the Circle, with the rest drawn from the hereditary lands or Silesia. He is against moving six regiments without provision. He notes that Halberstadt wants to be neutral.

He compares what other rulers maintain: the King of Denmark keeps some 8,000 men in Holstein, paid by the estates and Dithmarschen; Pomerania has 1,000 musketeers and 200 horse; Brunswick keeps troops summer and winter. He also mentions the archbishopric of Bremen, and England as unreliable. He sets out "three grades" of war in the Circle:
1. The Circle asks for the defence of the Mark (Brandenburg), which it cannot manage "ex ipsa imbecillitate petentium".
2. A promise under the imperial constitutions, but the Circle has no constitution ("sunt sine capite").
3. Neutrality: "inter duos litigantes tertius gaudet".

The imperial army is described as all but gone ("mit auss der Welt"), Hatzfeldt's included, and there is talk of retreat into Bohemia. If troops cannot be quartered in the Emperor's name, they could be quartered in the name of the Bishop of Halberstadt, with the Stift Halberstadt as a base. He warns that the enemy will not soon be seen out of Pomerania and that, without resolution and magnanimity, the Circle "and we will be lost together". The covering letter on p.7 regrets that he could not bring some regiments to the Elbe before the Kreisschluss, "for the army is quite ruined, et venter caret auribus".

## 3. Sample passages
1. [den Crais disjunct zu halten] "to keep the Circle disunited." This sits in the clear phrase "cum hoc unicum sit remedium".
2. [Halberstadt, Magdeburg, Hildesheim und Wolfenbüttel müssen uns im Crais ...] "Halberstadt, Magdeburg, Hildesheim and Wolfenbüttel must (keep) us in the Circle."
3. [die kaiserliche Armada ist mit auss der Welt, der Hatzfeld auch] "the imperial army is as good as gone from the world, Hatzfeldt's too." The last clause is part clear text.
4. [vel Caesaris vel episcopi nomine im Crais den Fuess zu erhalten] "to keep a foothold in the Circle in the name either of the Emperor or of the Bishop." Also [nomine episcopi Halberstadiensis im Crais losen] "quarter in the Circle in the name of the Bishop of Halberstadt."
5. [etliche Regimenter an die Elbe zu bringen ... aber die Armada ist gar ruinirt] "to bring some regiments to the Elbe ... but the army is quite ruined."

## 4. Confidence
About 60% of the cipher reads coherently, and nearly every place name and key noun reads. Many short fragments are corrupt or of uncertain sense: "getelcksolleab fuehren", "uhrnotturnhit", "teohinessichretriernsende", "tecian", "iesrckerie", "sosrcksein", "gestftgehetdaruberseugrungt", "ofseferiertsiichesnoch", "eintermaurseinmitdenapintenconiu", "icrhochfurstdurchlnimmertenschofson", "qundoqurterinprocaemre", "segneutralitusnonpetentezemptonemtu" and "unrenzusngt". A Latin phrase on p.6 seems to render "salus populi suprema lex esto" as "mluspopulipremalezestu". Because the text interleaves clear and cipher heavily, the syntax is often hard to follow even where the words read.
Reading

# R4625, Kurz von Senftenau to Trauttmansdorff, Hamburg, 10 Feb 1639

[...] zu heben. Will man sich dan lieber dem Crais als den Kaser und das Reich obligiren, cum hoc unicum sit remedium
et semper fuerit, den Crais disunctu zu halten, und dardurch Haus Sachsen gestrafen, so darunder dem ganzen Röm. Reich
zu begegnen: Halberstat, Magdeburg, Hildesheimb und Wolfenbuttel müssen uns im Crais und das Reich sein authoritet
erhalten, wan wir es selbst den wollen abandoniren. Selbst thun, das darvon ezcludiren mit wenig thun, was andere Stand
thun, ob hat es seinen weg, wie leicht thun an disen ortten zwai und dreitausendt man erhalten werden, wann die kopf
fur kopf verpflegt, das übrige aber von ihnen in [...] oder Schlesien empfangen würdt. Politice also darnach zuwerden,
so bin ich ganz und gar der meinung nit, das man [?dem] volck solle abfuehren, sondern mehrers [...] hinzusetzen,
dann sehs Regimenteren, aber die da seindt, ihr notturft, wann die an land die ein den esignierten ortten mit
empfangen thun, in den Erblanden reichlich geben, die Artigleria zu bestellen, wie es gehört.

Der haben wir im Halberstatittischen mit vermüthen, militariter darnach zuwerden, da würdt man wissen, ob das volck
bei aller weiteren [?einbruch] des feinds, und wohin es sich retriern [...], ob dann dem Hazfeld oder wo [...] auss dem
Reich gehen solle. Ist doch der schonste [?praetext] von der welt iezundt volck in Crais zu bringen, weilen der
feindt darinnen; so sein dan das Halberstat auch neutral wolle sein. In somma, ich weiss nichts anders zu rath, [?sa] vel
Caesaris vel episcopi nomine im Crais den fues zu erhalten, und ie starcker ie besser. Zwaihundert thun leicht für
tausent essen ohne die Canalia; so wollen wir bald so starck sein als Herzog Georg, und auch die letzt, [?der] stift
gehet daruber zu grundt, quod non fiet, oder weniger im Jahr und tag mit so erdurch Ihrer Kays. Mtt. des heyl. Röm.
Reichs [...]; wann tausent fur tausent essen [...].

[...] forcht und respect. Der König zu Dennemarckh als Herzog von Holstein helt in Holstein allezeit zur Not und
hierauss ein 8000 Mann; die Stände haben darmit Ihr Königl. Mt. contribuiert, und die Dithmarschen gegeben und werden es
noch thun, dass ist [?auch] wider ihre privilegia. Holstein hat die sicherheit und respect vom Frembdt ratione Regni
Danie; Halberstatt ist alle tag des feindts raub. Pommern hat 1000 Musketierer extraordinari, 200 Pferdt, die Standt
sein es mit schuldig zu halten. Si talibus conseliis voluimus uti, ad quid profuisset archiepiscopatus Bremensis? Braunschweig
halt ein anzahl volckh, hat Quartier vor ein Jahr darzu noch gegeben, [?offeriert] [?sich] es noch zu thuen, halt das
volckh sommer und winter: warumb kan Halberstat nit eben das thuen, und was [?dem] landt der Armada nit geben, der tacite von
Kai. Maiest empfangen, dardurch der religion, dem algemeinen weesen dienen, und gleichsamb ein vormaur sein mit
den [...] coniunction andere Kays. und Chur Sachsen ihre Völckher, Ihr Kays. Mt. Erblanden, beiden anderen Standen
respect erhalten, den feindt in gelosia gezogen, das er sich des Engelland nie versicheren thun? Ancipites pro Suecis
in gegen halten, titubantes pro Caesare animiren, und mit mal a proposito sich und das stift verliehren, bös consilia
dispensiren. Kriegen sie es einmahl in ein Crais berathschlagung, per modum eins Craisschlus, so sein [?ihr] hochfurst. durchl.
nimmer Bischof, sonder die Landtschafft und das Capitel. Ich sag: quando quartier in pro Caesare non possunt obtinere, quod
est consultius, das es für diessmahl geschehe und der andere [...] auf das andere Jahr gespahrt werde. Wir haben uns
drin mit vermuhet, Halberstat zu haben, dem [...] geist, dem Thumbcapitularen, dem [...] hof zuwarten, sonder ein fues
im Crais.

[...] Marckh angreiffen und a tempo thun, was ich im thun ist, quae nuper scripsi; sein zwar sachen, deren zum theill
bereit erfolgt, theils zubegegnen. Sed neutralitas non, petente ezemptionem [...], et sub hoc vocabulo
Salvius [...] neutralitati [...], et ipsimet forte petentes. Der hackhen wollen wir sehen ein stihl finden, wann es auch
dahin kame. Tres sunt gradus bey dem Crais krieg von dem Churmarckhen. 1°. Ut petat Circulus in defensione Marckh sine
distinctione, ob es im Crais oder Landtschafften seye, quae est is defensio? Si non [?possunt] defendere [?Lauenburgum],
[...] Lineburgum, also falt dis ex ipsa imbecillitate petentium. 2do. dem Crais versprechung per constitutiones Imperii:
ist ein Crais verfassung nit, dess so einer zu hauss [?sitze] und sein zaun huete; sunt sine capite, nolunt regem,
[...] non [...] alios, manco mal per noi; inter duos litigantes tertius gaudet. 3tio. petant neutralitatem: certe petendum est ab
illis, war dem ehe feindt, wer freundt seye; non se resoluent eodem [...] Caesarem [...] habere. Die kaiserliche
Armada ist mit auss der Welt, der Hazfeldt auch; et si stante etiam neutralitate, quae nulla est, donec Caesar
consentiat, religui sunt armati, nonne et nos possumus esse armati in den Halberstattischen, in dem dreistatten in
Honstein, und zwar vil ehr als so, wann sich solches tausendt pferdt effective in [?dem] Halberstattische, das der Stab
in Boheimb. Wir haben die ursach retraitte, eremus alijs [?formidabiles], und so viel weniger frist man in Boheimb; und
in Gottes Namen, essen sie davon und in Halberstat, quid tum? sie schimpfen darzu leben. Si nomine Caesaris
excludamur a quartieriis, khönden wir mit nomine episcopi Halberstadiensis im Crais sein, und sarcker als
niemandts, in dem die stäb zu hauss, die Landt und so viel weniger beschwerdt bleiben; ohne Reputation der guetten
disciplin werden wir darinen haben der religion und dero hochloblichen Erzhaus dienst, und es geschicht nichts, was
nit andirer fursten underthanen ihnen kommen sommer und winter empfinden. Wir werden doch den feindt nit in Pommeren so
bald sehen, und alle iahr ein armada hunger lassen sterben, und die [...] nach und nach consumiren, sondern den feindt von
weitem bloqurter halten, wan es auch vil iahr solte werren, biss die Standt der sach mued werden, und etwas
Dennemarck, weiss Gott, wan ein resolution fast. Das würdt diser ortten der krieg sein, ihrer hochfurst. durchl.
underthenigister getrewester diener bin ich gewiss nicht, als khönnen ein stift Halberstat und zu Wien darzu mit;
dieses aber allein der fructus und zwar der groste, den wir von Halberstat haben khönden; dann was mir
dessen mit geniesen will, so muss ich es dahin gestelt sein lassen und leben daheim. So bedaure ich mit unseren
zustandt, dass ihm mit moglich zu hellfen solte sein, sondern das zum theill die Standt gegen dem feindt, sonderlich
diser ortten, nicht genungsamb cooperieren [...], zum theill aber wir selbsten die mittel, so wir noch ubrig haben,
mit genungsamber resolution und magnanimitet nit ergreifen wollen. Conniventia et clementia allein durchzukomben ist
unmöglich: die wafen muesen respect machen. Der ein Jahr noch hat, man glaubt, wir verlangten khein frieden, sondern
ain und den andern Standt nach und nach zu schwechen; dann mehr glaubt khainer, der weg hat. Und wir haben das dem nit
also den Standen [...] mit Ungern und diss gemeinen Weesen gesehen, kein weiters testimonium zu geben, sondern es würdt
auf die letst muesen haisen: salus populi suprema lez estu. Und nit wir selbst der Crais iudicirt, sonderen wie
es Ihr Kays. Mt. bestimbten, es gehe daruber wie Got wolle; sonst würdt der Crais und wir [?verlohren] zugleich gehen.
Verbleibe. Hamburg den 10. Febr. Ao 1639. [monogram and signature]

[Covering letter, p. 7 (docketed 1639 Feb. 10):] Hochgeborner Herr, Gnädiger Herr. E. H. schreiben de dato den 22.
Jan. hab ich vorgestert empfangen und gestert beantworttet; hoff, es solle ankhommen. Was den 19. eiusdem khombt dises
Augenblickh, ist khurz; ich muss aber was lang darauf antwortten. Der gast ist schon hinüber, prout ex ultimis. Hett ich mit den
respect gehabt, von dem E. H. melden, so hett ich mich zu meiner verwehrung und reputation unterstanden, etliche
Regimenter an die Elbe zu bringen bis zum Craisschluss, damit sie besser die proviant in loco als [...] nit verzehrt
hetten. Wenn auch regimenter verhanden gewiesen, das es mit ein oder zwey hett versehen khönnen, so hett ich was gewagt;
aber die armada ist gar zu ruinirt, et venter caret auribus: sie haben fort gemuest. E. H. wollen sich vor dem
[?nidersachsischen] Crais nit furchten, wann man das [...]

## Notes

- Date: the memorandum on pp. 2-6 ends "Verbleibe. Hamburg den 10. Febr. Ao 1639" with a monogram and signature (p. 6,
  not transcribed before); the covering letter on p. 7 is docketed 1639 Feb. 10 and answers Trauttmansdorff's of 22 Jan.
- Signs: `9` is polyphonic (te, and vo in volck, vormaur, von, vor, voluimus, darvon: relabelled 9v, key 9v = vo);
  the crossed long-s is ta after 27 and in alle tag, tacite (relabelled #f-cross) and vi in proviant, privilegia,
  vil iahr, Salvius (relabelled pv, key pv = vi, 22 Oct); elsewhere it stays `p` (null). The cipher writes z for x
  (ezcludiren, ezemptionem, praetezt, lez estu) and 99 (= e) for de/dem (esignierten, de volck); printed as the figures
  give them.
- Recurrent misread fixed: `6 #z-tail` (sen) transcribed `62 #z-tail` (tausent, essen, geniesen, muesen, lassen
  sterben).
- Unread: the opening words of p. 2 (the memorandum begins mid-sentence); runs that do not decode into words
  ("mehrers etgentedi sertio", "in teinimb", "tecian", "eodem bi coCaesarem satequte bannieriam", "unlangen",
  "retriern sende", "dem behren geist", "dem massoni bauren hof"); Latin on p. 5 with run-together groups ("salus dt",
  "megablisa Lineburgum"); two words of the p. 7 clear text.
- The clear text of pp. 3 and 5 is the agents' fast reading of a cursive hand ("gestrafen", "manes mal per noi", "der
  ein Jahr noch hat"); pp. 2, 6 and 7 were re-read on the image in the second pass.
Translation

<!-- English translation of read/R4625_reading.md (Claude Opus 5.5, 22 Oct 2026, batch b06, second pass). Same holes:
[...] unread, [?word] uncertain, [word] supplied. -->

# R4625, Kurz von Senftenau to Trauttmansdorff, Hamburg, 10 Feb 1639: English

[...] to raise. If one would rather bind oneself to the Circle than to the Emperor and the Empire, since this is and
always has been the only remedy, to keep the Circle disunited and thereby [...] the House of Saxony, so as to meet
[danger] to the whole Roman Empire: Halberstadt, Magdeburg, Hildesheim and Wolfenbüttel must keep us in the Circle and
keep the Empire its authority, if we ourselves do not want to abandon it. To do it ourselves, to exclude [others] from
it with little, what other estates do: it can be done, how easily two and three thousand men can be kept in these
places, if they are fed head for head, and the rest is received from them in [...] or Silesia. Politically, then, I am
not at all of the opinion that one should withdraw the troops, but rather add more [...], than six regiments; but those
that are there [should] receive their necessities in the [?designated] places, be given plentifully in the hereditary
lands, the artillery be provided as it should be.

We have hopes in the Halberstadt country of becoming military; there one will know whether the troops, with any
further [?irruption] of the enemy and wherever it retreats [...], should then go to Hatzfeldt, or where [...], out of
the Empire. It is now the finest [?pretext] in the world to bring troops into the Circle, since the enemy is in it; so
they say then that Halberstadt too wants to be neutral. In sum, I know no other counsel than to keep a foothold in the
Circle in the name of either the Emperor or the bishop, and the stronger the better. Two hundred easily do for a
thousand, eat without the rabble; so we shall soon be as strong as Duke Georg, and at the last [?the] bishopric goes to
ruin over it, which will not happen, or less in a year and a day, with [...] His Imperial Majesty's and the Holy Roman
Empire's [...]; when a thousand eat for a thousand [...].

[...] fear and respect. The King of Denmark, as Duke of Holstein, always keeps in Holstein for need some 8,000 men; the
estates have contributed to His Royal Majesty for it, and the Dithmarschen have given and will still do so, which is
[?even] against their privileges. Holstein has security and respect from foreigners by reason of the Danish crown;
Halberstadt is every day the enemy's prey. Pomerania has 1,000 musketeers extraordinary and 200 horse, which the
estates are bound to keep. If we had wished to use such counsels, what would the archbishopric of Bremen have
profited? Brunswick keeps a number of troops, has given quarters for a year besides, [?offers] to do it still, keeps
the troops summer and winter: why can Halberstadt not do the same, and what does the [?country] not give the army,
which it tacitly received from His Imperial Majesty, thereby serve religion and the common weal, and be as it were a bulwark with
the [...] conjunction, other imperial and Electoral Saxon troops, His Imperial Majesty's hereditary lands, keep respect
for both the other estates, draw the enemy into jealousy, so that he can never be sure of England? To hold the
undecided against the Swedes, to encourage the wavering for the Emperor, and with ill timing lose themselves and the
bishopric, dispense bad counsels. If they once get it into a Circle deliberation, by way of a Circle resolution, then
His Highness is never bishop, but the estates and the chapter. I say: when quarters cannot be obtained for the
Emperor, it is wiser that it be done for this time and the other [...] be saved for the next year. We have hoped to
have Halberstadt, to wait on the [...], the cathedral canons, the [...] court, but [to keep] a foot in the Circle.

[...] attack the Mark and do in time what I [...], as I wrote lately; these are things of which some have already
happened, some are to be met. But neutrality not, with [them] asking exemption [...], and under this word Salvius [...]
neutrality [...], and perhaps those asking themselves. We shall see to find a handle for the axe, if it came to that.
There are three grades in the Circle war about the Electoral Mark. First: that the Circle ask for the defence of the
Mark without distinction whether it be in the Circle or the territories; what is this defence? If they cannot defend
[?Lauenburg], [...] Lüneburg, this falls by the very weakness of those asking. Second: the promise to the Circle under
the constitutions of the Empire: there is no Circle constitution, so that each one [?sits] at home and guards his fence; they are without a head, they want no king, [...] not [...] others, so much the better for us; between two quarrellers the third
rejoices. Third: let them ask for neutrality; certainly it must be asked of them, who is enemy before, who friend; they
will not resolve at the same [...] to have the Emperor [...]. The imperial army is as good as gone from the world,
Hatzfeldt's too; and if, even with the neutrality standing, which is none, until the Emperor consents, the rest are
armed, can we not also be armed in the Halberstadt country, in the three towns in Hohnstein, and indeed much
sooner than if such a thousand horse [are] effectively in [?the] Halberstadt country, while the staff [is] in Bohemia.
We have cause [for] retreat, we shall be [?formidable] to others, and so much less does one eat in Bohemia; and in
God's name, let them eat from it, and in Halberstadt, what then? They scoff at living from it. If in the Emperor's
name we are excluded from quarters, we can be in the Circle in the name of the bishop of Halberstadt, and [?stronger]
than anyone, since the staffs stay at home and the country is so much less burdened; without the reputation of good
discipline we shall have in it the service of religion and of the most praiseworthy Archducal House, and nothing
happens that the subjects of other princes do not feel coming to them summer and winter. We shall not see the enemy
out of Pomerania so soon, and every year let an army starve to death, and the [...] consume little by little, but keep
the enemy blockaded from afar, even if it should last many years, until the estates grow tired of the matter, and
something [from] Denmark, God knows when it takes a resolution. That will be the war in these parts. His Highness's most
humble, most faithful servant I am certainly not, as a bishopric of Halberstadt can, and at Vienna besides; but this
alone the fruit, and indeed the greatest, which we could have from Halberstadt; for what of it will profit me, I must
leave it at that and live at home. So I regret our state, that it should be possible to help him, but that partly the
estates do not cooperate enough against the enemy, especially in these parts [...], partly that we ourselves will not
take up the means we still have with enough resolution and magnanimity. To get through with connivance and clemency
alone is impossible: arms must make respect. [...] one believes we wish no peace, but to weaken one estate after
another; for no one who has the way believes more. And we have [...] the estates [...] with Hungary and this common
weal, to give no further testimony, but in the end it will have to be: the safety of the people is the supreme [?law].
And not we ourselves judged the Circle, but as His Imperial Majesty decides, let it go as God wills; otherwise the
Circle and we will go [?lost] together. I remain. Hamburg, 10 February 1639. [monogram and signature]

[Covering letter, p. 7 (docketed 1639 Feb. 10):] Well-born lord, gracious lord. Your Excellency's letter dated 22
January I received the day before yesterday and answered yesterday; I hope it will arrive. What comes of the 19th of
the same at this moment is short; but I must answer something long to it. The guest is already over, as from the last
[letters]. Had I had the respect Your Excellency mentions, I would have undertaken, for my defence and reputation, to
bring some regiments to the Elbe until the Circle resolution, so that they could better have provisions on the
spot than consume the [...]. Even if regiments had been at hand, so that it could have been provided with one or two, I
would have ventured something; but the army is quite too ruined, and the belly has no ears: they had to go. Your
Excellency need not fear the [?Lower Saxon] Circle, if one [...]

R4645, Hamburg, 13 Feb 1639

The Danish interposition and a possible conjunction with the Emperor: the King's distinctions proved mere lies; Count Pentz says the King must drop the mediation and denounce Sweden. With clear copies of two letters of Banér of 29 Jan 1639.

Transcription
