R4645_clear.md

# R4645: clear-text pages (P3, P4, P5, P7, P8; clear lines on P6 and P9)

## Summary

R4645 holds the Hamburg cipher letter of 13 Feb 1639 (P9, P1, P2, P6) and, as enclosures, **copies of two letters
by the Swedish field marshal Johan Banér (signed "Johan Banner")**. Both concern the Swedish army's quarters in the
Lower Saxon Circle and the town of Lüneburg.

- **Banér letter A, 10 Jan 1639** (P7, continued on P5 left). To the burgomasters and council of Lüneburg. Dated
  "Neuhauß" [?]. Banér recalls that in August 1636 Lüneburg submitted to the Swedish Crown, and in 1637 broke away,
  drove the royal garrison out of the town "mit allerhandt hostiliteten" and seized the Kalkberg. He says he
  could hold this against them as enemies but asks them to let his army and allies pass through the Lüneburg
  territory and to let his men buy provisions, bread, beer and salt in the town for cash, instead of turning them
  away with hostile words. If they refuse, he will give no further warning.
- **Banér letter B, 29 Jan 1639** (P3, continued on P8 left; endorsement on P4). To the envoys of the princes and
  estates of the Lower Saxon Circle assembled at Lüneburg, answering their letter of 25 Dec [1638] brought by a
  trumpeter. They asked him to take his army out of its present quarters and out of the whole Circle. Banér
  replies that the Circle's own principals have been arming against the Swedish Crown, that he offered
  reconciliation which was refused, that he must use his arms against those who show hostility, and that he
  cannot leave the Circle while that posture continues; he will abide by what is fitting. Dated "auf dem
  Closter zu [Alden?]stett" [?], 29 Jan 1639.
- **P6 / P9, the Hamburg letter of 13 Feb 1639**: the clear lines are the closing of the cipher letter (partly
  Italian/Latin, very cursive hand), the date "Hamburg 13 Feb. 1639", a signature, and a four-line postscript
  marked #. P9 carries only a salutation line.

Note on page order: the existing tr/ notes pair P3 with P5 and P7 with P8. The text runs the other way: P7 ends
"welche zu ihrem be-" and P5 begins "hueff, Proviant ..."; P5 is dated 10 Jan (the archival note on P7 reads
"1639 Jan. 10."), and P8 is dated 29 Jan (the note on P3 reads "1639 Jan. 29."). So A = P7+P5, B = P3+P8.

Legibility: the two Banér copies are in a clear chancery hand and are read almost completely; roughly 15-20 words
are marked [?] and none wholly illegible. The P6 closing and postscript are in a fast personal hand: about half
the words are doubtful and several are [...].

Conventions: line breaks as in the MS; `/` for the virgule; abbreviations kept as written (`Königl:`, `Mt:`,
`H.`); u/v and long s normalised to u/v and s; [?] = doubtful reading; [...] = illegible.

---

## Letter A: Banér to Lüneburg, 10 Jan 1639

### P7 (archival note top left: "1639 Jan. 10.")

    Edle Ehrnveste, Hochgelehrte und Wohlweise [?], Besondere [?],
    gute [?] liebe Herren und Freunde.

    Es wirdt den Herren annoch erinnerlich sein, Und in guetem gedacht-
    nis stehen, auf waß maße im Augusto, des 1636. Jahres, die
    Stadt [?] Lüneburg [?] Ihr Königl: May(t): und Eron Schweden Devotion
    sich verbindtlich gemacht, und ihre etliche Jahr vorhero verbeuti- [?]
    gene reale Submission, in Ihr Königl: May(t): und Eron Schweden
    schutz ernewert, auch deßelben würcklich genoßen, aber baldt
    darauf, und in folgendem 1637. Jahre sich gefallen laßen,
    ohne alle Ursache, aus solcher Verbündtnus abzuspringen, die
    Königl: Guarnison auß ihrer Stadt, mit allerhandt hostili-
    teten beznüsungen [?], ab: und auß dem Kalckberg zu schaffen,
    welche alsobaldt darauf von denen, nicht mit weinigen [?],
    der Herren und Ihrer Burgerschafft zu [...]  [?], auch angetrie-
    ben worden, derinnen [?] ihnen des Commendanten faute, daß
    [...] auch gehöriger maßen gelitten, vermeintlich zu statten ko-
    men,   So ich nun wohl vermeint, es würden die-
    selbe [?] Herren seit deme ihr gewißen [?] Examiniret, und sich solche
    actiones haben leidt sein laßen, so befinde ich doch mit
    sonderbahrer befrembdung, das Contrarium so sehr, daß nach-
    dem ich mit der mir anvertrauten Armee, par raison de
    guerre und den offenbahren feindt, Ihr Königl: May(t):
    und Eron Schweden, und deren Allyirten zu verfolgen, mich
    derumb [?] aber die Elbe zugehen, und des Fürstl: Lüneburg[ischen]
    gischen [sic] territorium im vorbey passiren zu betretten ge-
    nöthiget worden, die von denen, welche zu ihrem be-

### P5 left (continuation)

    hueff, Proviant und bedürfftige wahren, in ihrer Stadt
    einzukauffen gemeinet, ihnen zu verhalten [?], und mit feind-
    seligen worten, ja die ihnen nichts anders als hostili-
    teten anbieten laßen, abweisen,

    Will derowegen die Herren hiermit ermahnet haben, die wollen
    Considerieren, daß eine so weitläufftige Armee, nicht ohne brodt
    und dazu gehörige Lebensmittell fortgeführet werden
    kan, und dannenhero so wohl einen iedern der es mit discre-
    tion begehrt, nicht allein vor sein geldt seine notturfft
    einkauffen laßen, sondern auch solche Verfügung thun,
    damit aus ihrer Stadt, der Armee, mit einem erklecklich[en]
    [?] an Brodt, Bier, und Saltz beygesprungen, und also derosel-
    ben fortzug so viel mehr beschleunigt, bis man lenger [?]
    ohne [?] Ursach [?], als ich sonst gesonnen, in ihrer gegend auf-
    gehalten, und sie deren Inconvenientien in Commoditet [?] remoriret [?]
    Ihnen selbst Ungelegenheit uf den halß gezogen, und durch
    einen solchen resolution, der Ich gewärtig sein [?] mich [?] ent-
    halten wolte, Commoniret werden möge.   Wie ich sie
    [?] mich zu den Herren und Ihrer Stadt Einwohnern
    verbleibe [?] versehe [?], Deß will ich bey Zeigern, diesen
    Trompeter, Ihrer gewierigen [?] antwort und bestendige [?]
    Unverzüglich gewertig sein, Und des den [?] schleunige auße-
    lung [?] hiermit gebethen haben, Thue die H. damit der
    Schutz des höchsten empfehlen,    Datum Neuhauß den 10.
                                       January Ao. 1639.
    Den H[erren] [?]                     Williger Freundt
    Burgermeister und
    Rath der Stadt Lüneburg.                 Johan Banner.

## Letter B: Banér to the Lower Saxon Circle envoys at Lüneburg, 29 Jan 1639

### P3 (archival note top left: "1639 Jan. 29.")

    Wohledle, Gestrenge, Ehrnveste, Achtbare [?] und
    Hochgelehrte, Insonders günstige liebe Herren
    und Freunde,

    Der Herren am 25. Decemb. in Lüneburg an mich außgefertigte
    schreiben, hat mir der abgeschickte Trompet
    zu recht eingeliefert, Und habe daraus die Summam
    dahin gerichtet gesehen, daß man mich gleichsamb gebetten
    will [?] meine unterhabende Armee nicht nur von denen
    orthen da sie ietzt ist, sondern gar von des gantzen
    Nieder Sächsischen Craißes Landen abzuführen, Und
    [?] zwar [?] auf einige gewechselte schreiben, ob gleich
    wohl mir deß Fürstl: Hauß Braunschweig und Lüne-
    burg angelegen [?], Und auch derselben noch zur Zeit keine [?]
    schließ gemacht [?], zu verwirren [?] gedencket,   Die
    weil [?] sie die Herren wie deren principalen mehrern theils
    Ihre offentliche Waffen wider Ihr Königl: Mt: und
    Eron Schweden [...] kehren [?] und Ihre Wohlfahrt wohl
    meritirten Kriegsheer bestellen [?] employrt,
    und damit der Catholischen oder Ligisten armatur
    gegen dieselben feindtlich beyspringen können, dies
    nicht unterlaßen, Und zu solchem Werckh noch
    feindtlich all Ihr vermögen anwenden und ergeben [?]
    offeriren, Also daß es keines mehrern außführens
    bedarff, sondern weltkündig ist, Kombt mir
    auch desto weniger sehr befrembdet für, daß die

### P8 left (continuation)

    mein gutes Anerbietten auf alle die gewünschte [?] einige
    reconciliation mit Ihr Königl: Mt: und hochlöbl:
    Eron Schweden, keinen Anlaß geben, abstringiren [?]
    wollen, Und mich zu übereylen gedencken, Ich
    müste die Waffen von denen die alle hostilitet gegen
    die mir anvertraute Armada exerciren, ab-
    kehren, Und alle Ihre Proceduren freundtlich auf-
    nemen, mich auch gegen die als einen Freundt com-
    portiren,   Weil aber solches wider alle
    gebühr, insonderheit auch natürliche defensions
    Rechte lauffen wolte, So werden die Herren mich
    entschuldiget [?] haben [?], daß Ich meinen bezeigungen
    nach Ihrer principalen contenance richten
    und Ihrer angetragenen freundtschafft so lange
    die nicht beygelegt würdt, entgegen arbeiten
    muß,   Was aber gegen einen und andern
    freindtliebenden Standt dieses löbl: Craißes, auß
    geschehener veranlaßung von mir anerbotten,
    wurde Ich praestitis praestandis nicht verän-
    dern, sondern allen freündt: und freundtlichen be-
    zeigungen nach, deren gebühr und verhoffenden
    Zubegegnen gefließen bleiben,   Thue die Herren
    damit der Göttlichen Obhacht empfehlen,  Dat:
    auf dem Closter zu [Alden?]stett [?], den 29.
                                        January 1639.
          D. H.                   Williger Freundt,

                                   Johan Banner.

<!-- Reading of R4645 (the cipher letter), built 22 Oct 2026 by Claude Opus 5.5 (REREAD_BRIEF, batch b06). It rests
on the key decode (key.json with 38 = d) of tr/IMG_R4645_I27398_P9, P1, P2, P6 (the letter's order) after the
corrections in read/R4645_corrections.tsv, the cipher lines checked on the DECODE images. No contemporary
decipherment, gloss or edition. The enclosed Banér copies (P3, P4, P5, P7, P8) are clear text, read in
read/R4645_clear.md, and are not repeated here. Letters in brackets inside a word ([nu], [su]) are the values of the
signs Δ and #dot that the key does not have (proposals, see Notes). Marks: [...] unread; [?word] partly carried;
[word] supplied. Mixed German and Latin, as written. -->

# R4645, Kurz von Senftenau to Trauttmansdorff, Hamburg, 13 Feb 1639

[Salutation, one line in Kurz's hand, not read.]

Graf Penz sagt mir, war initium coniunctionis futurum: das sich der konig der interposition entschlage und dises
der Cron Schweden denuncire, und darbei das sie in mora tractatuum eines und das andere werde, er [?zu]gleich
nit thuen. Ergo ruptura tractatuum hic est principium coniunctionis. Lasse es dahingestelt sein; bleibt in nostris
consiliis bei dem, was iungst geschriben. Saepius [?dixi] [...] quuam: wan man bis dato nit begrife, de aus
den tractatibus nichts konne und wolle werden, so hab ich die capacitet nit, mit mehrerem es zu deduciren;
consequenter sich regi [?persuasio] officia potest esse spes congressus, quae pacis; ipsa confessione
confaederationis indictum est illi bellum; sumus itaque et[i]am clare [?inclusis] tractatibus. Das ihm der konig
leicht die raitungen machen: die wahl, wan er sich nit movirt, mues springen uber [?kurz] oder lang. Noch ein brief
wil ich ihm schreiben; postea dies interpellabit pro homine. Umb gottes willen, man [?zeig] dem Nuncio die zahn,
die absurditates et voluntatem rumpendi; [?wollen] schon, nichts geschicht. E. Gna. glauben mir, es wurdt vil
helfen ad promotionem. Ich dencke nun auf ein weeg, wie zu praeveniren, [?da] der Kaiser und das Reich auf kein
coniunction zu verlassen; wir wollen uns dannoch und auch allein wehren. In was fur ein laborint der konig iezundt,
und wie er [?drin]steckht, de[s] sehen E. G.; er mag nun heraus kommen wie er wolle.

Quicquid Salveus pro fundanda regis interpositione distinctionibus suis, sine quibus [?rex] noluit suscipere
interpositionem, spopondit, sunt pura puta mendacia, toties a me regi praedicta. Des konigs interposition [...]
felt respectu illorum omnium quae e[x] parte Gallorum sunt necessaria ad congressum, un[d] de summa tractatuum iam et
hic dependet. Quae ergo [...]

[The text breaks here: P2 ends "quae ergo" and P6 begins mid-sentence; a leaf between them is not among the DECODE
images.]

[...] Ohsenstern nit abermal die prorogationem faederis in Suecia durchtringe, zu der sich das reich nur ein iahr
noch bekent. Et mittam prozime meine gedancken, aber allein ewer gnaden, bey einer pantalonada. Der Herzog ist also
[...] wie zu Wien in bono [...]: [...] apparet. Befehle mich [...].

Hamburg 13. Feb. 1639. [Signature.]

[Postscript in Kurz's cursive, four lines, not read.]

## Notes

- Δ reads **nu** here and in every other letter (de-nu-ncire, Nuncio, nun twice; elsewhere warnung, hoffnung,
  continuieren, eigennutz, "nur wolle" in R3811); key.json corrected (was e), 22 Oct 2026.
- #dot reads **su** (here none in the cipher lines read; in the other letters sufficient, subsidia, consummirter,
  summum periculum, succurs, Suecos); the key treats it as a null.
- φ (z) on P2 l. 8: the sign transcribed #sect is φ on the image ("iezundt").
- "?(8742)" on P2 l. 8: the figures before "nsteckht" are not settled ("drin" by sense).
- "[?kurz]", "[?zeig]": the figures give #sect (w) where the sense needs z (ψ/φ); "[?zu]gleich" likewise.
- Salveus: the figures spell Salvius thus.
- Open: the missing leaf between P2 and P6; the closing lines and postscript in Kurz's cursive on P6 (image blocker,
  recorded before); the Latin run "saepius di[?x]i pen wi o a ut en cau ten quam" (P9 ll. 17-18).

# R4645, Kurz von Senftenau to Trauttmansdorff, Hamburg, 13 Feb 1639

[Salutation not read.]

Count Penz tells me [?it was] the beginning of the future conjunction that the King should give up the mediation and
[?denounce] this to the Crown of Sweden, and moreover that while the negotiations are delayed the one and the other
would happen, he [?also] would not do it. So the rupture of the negotiations is here the beginning of the
conjunction. I leave it undecided; our counsels stay with what I wrote lately. I have [?said] often [...]: if one does
not grasp by now that nothing can or will come of the negotiations, then I do not have the capacity to argue it
further; consequently [...] persuaded the King [...] good offices can be the hope of the congress, which [is the hope]
of peace; by the very admission of the confederation war has been declared on him; we are therefore now clearly
[?shut out] of the negotiations. That the King can easily do the reckoning: the choice, if he does not move, must
come sooner or later. I will write him one more letter; afterwards the days will plead for the man. For God's sake,
let one [?show] the Nuncio one's teeth, [and] the absurdities and the will to break off; [...], nothing happens. Your
Grace may believe me, it would help much towards progress. I am now thinking of a way to forestall [?that] the Emperor
and the Empire [should] rely on no conjunction; we will still defend ourselves, even alone. In what a labyrinth the
King now is, and how he [?is] stuck in it, Your Grace sees; let him come out of it as he will.

Whatever Salvius promised to found the King's mediation with his distinctions, without which [?the King] would not
undertake the mediation, are pure lies, so often foretold to the King by me. The King's mediation [...] fails in
respect of all those things on the French side that are necessary for the congress, and on which the sum of the
negotiations already depends here too. Which therefore [...]

[A leaf is missing between P2 and P6.]

[...] Oxenstierna does not again push through the prolongation of the alliance in Sweden, to which the realm commits
itself for only one more year. And I will send my thoughts shortly, but to Your Grace alone, with a pantalonata [a
buffoonery]. The Duke is thus [...] as at Vienna in good [...]: [...] appears. I commend myself [...].

Hamburg, 13 Feb. 1639. [Signature.]

[Postscript in Kurz's cursive, four lines, not read.]

R4734, 8 Apr 1639, copy

The Danish mediation has expired and the King has his hands tied; the Lower Saxon Circle must contribute to war or to compensation; Stralsund or other lands might go to Brandenburg. Addressed to 'E. Excell.' (Schwarzenberg per DECODE).

R4734_endorsement.md

# R4734: endorsement (IMG_R4734_I27761_P3)

## Summary

Chancery endorsement on the outer leaf: a **copy of a letter from the Imperial Vice-Chancellor (Reichsvizekanzler,
i.e. Ferdinand Sigismund Kurz von Senftenau himself) to Count [von] Schwarzenberg** (presumably Adam von
Schwarzenberg, the Brandenburg minister), dispatched **8 April 1639**. Letter "A" of a series. The tr/ file read
the addressee as "Herrn Kurzen von Senftenau(?)"; the image reads "Herrn Graven [?] von Schwartzen-berg". Same
endorsing hand as R4645 P4. Nothing illegible; one word doubtful.

Conventions: line breaks as in the MS; abbreviations as written; [?] = doubtful.

## Transcription

    Copia
    deß Herrn Reichs Vice Canz-
    lers schreiben
    Ahn
    Herrn Graven [?] von Schwartzen-
    berg abgangen.
    8. April 1639.
    [flourish]
    A.

The top half of the page is blank.

R4734_reading.md

# R4734: Kurz von Senftenau (Imperial Vice-Chancellor) to Count Schwarzenberg, copy, 8 April 1639: reading of the cipher runs in their clauses

Running text of `read/R4734.txt` (cipher runs in [brackets] there, decoded unspaced; clear German in {braces}),
written out 23 Oct 2026 (translation wave v7): word division, capitals and punctuation added to the cipher runs, which
are given in ⟨ ⟩; letters as decoded (ezpirante, espiriert, unintrichierter, Stralndt as the figures give them).
**An excerpt**: about 70 lines of clear German round the runs are not transcribed in the repository; "…" marks them, as
in R4734.txt. `[?]` = a run or word not made out; `[?word]` = doubtful. German with Latin and Italian phrases.

## Reading

*Image P2.* … hat mir … bei … tractat … nichts … und nichts will … und dahero mit denselben zu conferiren verlangt, was
⟨ezpirante interpositione fur ein weeg zu ergreifen⟩, zu dem lieben Frieden.

*Image P4, left.* … darüber nichts vernemben lassen können, ⟨bis iede forma coniunctionis versichert. Dis seie das⟩ mag …
und allein in Ihr Mitteln. Also will man sich aber … selbigen sache nit erklären, und also verursacht das … sich
selbsten: ⟨der konig bleibt der sachen unintrichierter⟩, und haben ⟨wir qutescunque [?] amplectendi nunmehr freie
handt⟩, und man … sich deshalben mit beschwerung, ⟨das man sat[?] literpraeteriert seie⟩. Ich versichere mich, das …
soll durch E. Excell. nichts anders werden zuschreiben, als … den 8. April 1639.

*Image P5.* … bey ich ganz und gar nichts mit E. Excell. …lich, daß ⟨die interposition zu ter [?] muese⟩ erreichen; zum
Andern, was … wegen Vermittlung gemeldt worden: ⟨die interposition ist schon espiriert; der konig sagt, er hab
gebundene hande und konne bei der sachen nichts mehr thuen; per force will er nichts thuen⟩. Also hält dieses
impedimentum, damit … und ihre … per tractaten sind. ⟨Ich glaube, iener [?] landt lieber in anderen als in sein
eigenen handen⟩. Der effectus neutralita- … sich ad casum feudi solle schreiben, … als 3 fumi, … mit … termino
reluitionis … wolle; ⟨die nidersachs craiss sindt fridt haben, so werden sie oder zum krieg oder [?] recompens
contribuiren⟩. Ich besorge mir dessen auch nit … Dessen statt ⟨Stralndt etwan hessische oder andere gueter der
iehnigen, so doch bei den Franzoli an noch verharren werden,⟩ Churfürstl. Dhlt. ⟨eingeraumbt wurden, halte es auch fur
billich⟩.

R4734_en.md

# R4734: Kurz von Senftenau to Count Schwarzenberg, copy, 8 April 1639: English translation of the cipher runs in their clauses

Translation (Claude Opus 5.5, 23 Oct 2026) of `read/R4734_reading.md`, **an excerpt**: the deciphered runs (in ⟨ ⟩)
with the clear clauses round them; "…" marks clear German not transcribed in the repository. Same holes: `[?]` a run
or word not made out. The endorsement (`read/R4734_endorsement.md`) reads: "Copy of the Lord Imperial Vice-Chancellor's
letter to the Lord Count [?] von Schwarzenberg, sent 8 April 1639. A."

## Translation

*Image P2.* … has [told] me … at … treaty … nothing … and will nothing … and therefore desired to confer with them what
⟨way to take, the interposition having expired⟩, towards dear peace.

*Image P4, left.* … could let nothing be heard about it, ⟨until every form of conjunction is assured. Let this be what⟩
may … and only in their means. Thus, however, one will not declare oneself … the same matter, and so causes that …
itself: ⟨the King remains the more [?unintrigued] in the matter⟩, and ⟨we now have a free hand to embrace as often as
[?]⟩, and one … therefore with complaint ⟨that one has been passed over [?] [?]⟩. I am sure that … nothing else shall
be ascribed by Your Excellency than … the 8th of April 1639.

*Image P5.* … in which I [have] nothing at all with Your Excellency …, that ⟨the interposition must [?] reach⟩; secondly,
what was reported … concerning the mediation: ⟨the interposition has already expired; the King says he has his hands
tied and can do nothing more in the matter; by force he will do nothing⟩. So this impediment holds, so that … and
their … are by treaties. ⟨I believe that [?] [would rather have the] land in others' hands than in his own⟩. The
effect of neutrality … should write itself ad casum feudi [for the case of the fief], … as 3 smokes [households], …
with … the term of redemption … would; ⟨the Lower Saxon Circle are [to] have peace, so they will contribute either to
the war or [?] to compensation⟩. I do not fear that either … Instead of it, ⟨[if] Stralsund, perhaps, [or] Hessian or
other lands of those who still persist with the French,⟩ [were] ⟨granted⟩ [to] His Electoral Highness, ⟨I hold it also
for fair⟩.

R4736, Glückstadt, 14 Apr 1639 read in part

At the Danish court: the Danes complain that war in Austria is bought with peace in Lower Saxony while the Swedes stay in Mecklenburg and Pomerania; papers to be sent to Schwarzenberg; outward respect for the Emperor nowhere greater than at Glückstadt.

Builds coherent sentences, but under the read bar of 95% of words read as sense (94% here); the holes are marked in the reading.

Transcription

R4736_reading.md

<!-- Reading of R4736, built 22 Oct 2026 by Claude Opus 5.5 (REREAD_BRIEF, batch b06). It rests on the key decode
(key.json with 38 = d) of tr/IMG_R4736_I27769_P1 and P3 after the corrections in read/R4736_corrections.tsv, every
cipher line checked on the DECODE images. No contemporary decipherment, gloss or edition. German, Latin and Italian
mixed, as written; spelling of the figures kept. Letters in brackets inside a word are values of signs the key lacks
or has otherwise (Δ = nu, #dot = su, the small x-like signs = su / vi / ta, #hash = x; see Notes); #s-long is printed
ti where the word needs it (the R3811 interlinear glosses it ti). Marks: [...] unread; [?word] partly carried; [word]
supplied. -->

# R4736, Kurz von Senftenau to Trauttmansdorff, Glückstadt, 14 Apr 1639

Hochgeborner Herr Graf,

Von hinnen wolt man mich nit gern dimittiren; mal sadisfatto et sadisfatto ist wider alle ihre principia. Quid
consilii [...] armorum zu [...] die massima wollen E. Chf. Dhl. [...] pro semper haben: das krieg in Osterreich fur
fridt in Nidersachsen gehalten wurdte, an schon die Schweden in Meckelburg und Pommeren bleiben. Optimo iure konden
wir unser allergnedigste herrschaft bitten, sie wolle uns [...] Pommeren nit verderben lassen. Quid erit, wan wir
alles gethan haben? Reditus nostri militis in Austriam vel no[vu]m bellum in armatos [...] imperii, et qui se tum cum
[?Suecis] coniungent? Fur solche vormauren geben wir unser schweis und bluet, hersezen alles in con
promess[e], [...] wir haben. Postqum nobis a [S]vecis penitus ener[v]atis omnia [...] nec contenti erunt, nec [Su]ecos etiam
tum tenent vel poterunt e[x] imperio eicere.

Hildesheimb und Glucks[ta]t ist eines: iener schiest den bolz, diser fangt ihne. Pen[?z] ist der gescheidest in
dissimuliren; alli altri [?scoppa] mi pensieri. Eines macht alhier gedancken, id est die lateinische scharteken:
[vi]det re[x] ingratitudinem, iam nos cogere ut rationes pacis ineamus, indem etiam cum praeiudicio [...]. Ich hab mich
nie nichts weiter daruber heraus gelassen; sie sich nichts super puncto coniunctionis. Also beruehet alles alhie,
ob sich selbst interposito regis [...], wan alle andere weeg verhaust, und wir i[m] sinn haben, das werck zu
accommodiren simul et interessati. Dan ist darvon wurden interim [...] solte man dem Kaiser kein satisfaction ganz und gar
nit geben, kein ocasion haben mit eim anderen officios, scheinbarlich das romische reich ein hofnung einziges vom
konig erfo[l]genden gueten die[n]sts; so mochten bede wol auf der Weser und Elbe das [...] rerum und die
authoritet mitlerweil springen. Negotium [...] dignum esse; daruber alle reichsrathe vernomben werden, ich auch
sub spe non futurae pacis.

Brandeburgicus ad aliquid est disponendus; Schwarzenbergio transmittenda; transmissa [su]nt, umb so [?vil] mehr,
weil Arnheimb [vi]lleicht das lateinische nach Khinsperg mochte haben, qui in itinere [...] regi. Hof, sie sollen
durch komben. Der eusserliche respect ist ninderst groser gegen Ihr Kay. Mtt. als zu [?Gluckstatt]; [...] iudicanda
e[x] factis. Wie hat sich der guete herr von Stralendorf seeliger betrogen! Glaub, sie wunschten ich [?were] nie der enden
gewesen; [...] alzeit solle es Ihr Kay. Mtt. nuzen. Arnheimb hat den pas von Ihr Kay. Mtt. durch mich nit
empfangen; ihn [?zu] obligiren, das er stil size, ist unmoglich: kan zu wasser und lande ohn pasport wo er hin will.

Glückstatt den 14. April 1639. [Signature.]

[Postscript in Kurz's cursive, bottom left, about six lines ("... 9 Martij ..."), and a margin note, not read.]

## Notes

- Key corrected 22 Oct 2026 for all letters (parent session, after kwic checks): Δ = nu (dignum, nuzen, hoffnung),
  #dot = su (sub spe), #s-long = ti, 38 = d, m = a. Still proposals: #sect = z
  (Penz, zu obligiren; in R3811 the interlinear glosses #sect w); #hash = x (ex imperio, ex factis); #s-long = ti
  (militis, optimo, satisfaction, negotium, interessati, stil, contenti); the small x-like sign stands for su
  (Suecos, transmissa sunt), vi (videt, villeicht, vil) and ta (Gluckstatt) as well as for i: several signs are merged
  under x in the transcription.
- The aligner (b06_align.py, #s-long = t) flags etiam (twice), satisfaction, negotium and stil: carried only
  under #s-long = ti.
- α in "i[m] sinn" (P3 l. 21) is the looped m-sign on the image; relabelled #a-loop.
- Open: the Latin and Italian runs that do not resolve ("quid consilii cu r • i armorum zu w u ki n", "vel no • i m
  bellum in armatis sitis imperii", "penitus e ne r m ti s omnia e #hash ti r se ri n t", "alli altri scoppa",
  "Bruzella mobla", "dan ist da r te n w u re de n interim dubi i n t", "lentra i gnadi", "qui in itinere re m le di
  ti t regi"); they need the sign values above settled first. The postscript and margin note in Kurz's cursive.
- Second pass (parent session, 22 Oct 2026, crops of P1 and P3): "vel no[vu]m bellum", "penitus ener[v]atis omnia", "ich
  [?were] nie der enden gewesen", "einziges vom konig erfo[l]genden gueten die[n]sts", "Dan ist darvon wurden",
  "regi. Hof, sie sollen durch komben", "re[x]" (#hash = x). 9 reads vo (key 9v, 22 Oct) in vom, von (twice), vormauren, darvon; relabelled 9v.
Translation

R4736_en.md

<!-- English translation of read/R4736_reading.md (22 Oct 2026, Claude Opus 5.5, b06). Same holes; Latin and Italian
translated. -->

# R4736, Kurz von Senftenau to Trauttmansdorff, Glückstadt, 14 Apr 1639

Most high-born Lord Count,

They would not gladly let me go from here; ill satisfied and satisfied is against all their principles. What counsel
[...] of arms to [...] the maxim His Electoral Highness wants [...] to hold for ever: that the war in Austria is held
to be peace in Lower Saxony, [even] though the Swedes stay in Mecklenburg and Pomerania. With the best right we
could ask our most gracious lordship not to let Pomerania be ruined for us [...]. What will happen when we have done
everything? The return of our soldiers to Austria, or a new war in the armed [...] of the Empire, and those who then
join the [?Swedes]? For such bulwarks we give our sweat and blood, stake everything in compromise [...] we have. After
the Swedes have thoroughly exhausted us, [...] everything, [they] will not be content, nor do they even now hold the Swedes
[?back], or could drive them out of the Empire.

Hildesheim and Glückstadt are one: the one shoots the bolt, the other catches it. Penz is the cleverest at
dissembling; to the others [...] my thoughts. One thing makes thoughts here, namely the Latin trash: the King sees the
ingratitude, [that it] now forces us to enter into terms of peace, while even with prejudice [...]. I have never let
anything further out about it; they [say] nothing about the point of the conjunction. So everything here rests on
whether, with the King [as] mediator, [...] when all other ways are blocked and we have it in mind to settle the
business and the interested parties together. Then from that [it has] meanwhile become [...]; one should give the Emperor no satisfaction
at all, have no occasion with another's good offices, apparently the Roman Empire a single hope of good service following from the King; so both might well [...] the [...] of affairs on the Weser and the Elbe, and the authority meanwhile go
to pieces. The business [...] worthy; all the councillors of the realm are heard on it, I too, in the hope of a peace
that will not come.

Brandenburg is to be brought to something; to be sent on to Schwarzenberg; [they] have been sent, all the more
because Arnim may perhaps have the Latin [paper] at Königsberg, who on the way [...] the King. I hope they will
get through. Outward respect for His Imperial Majesty is nowhere greater than at [?Glückstadt]; [...] to be judged
by deeds. How the good Lord von Stralendorf of blessed memory deceived himself! I think they wished I [?had] never been in these parts; [...] always it should profit His Imperial Majesty. Arnim has not received the pass from His Imperial Majesty
through me; to bind him to sit still is impossible: he can go by water and land without a passport wherever he wants.

Glückstadt, 14 April 1639. [Signature.]

[Postscript and margin note in Kurz's cursive, not read.]

Key: key.json, KEY.md

He describes the system, reports the key rebuilt from R3811 and summarises R3811. He prints no key or text.
